READ  IT  THE  WAY  TOPPERS  DO
How to use this book
One day a sitting. Read the day, then close the book and say the Five to remember out loud before you
look. That single habit is the difference between reading current affairs and scoring from them.
1
Read the day
Every item, source and
table. Underline the bold
facts: they are the exam.
2
Say the five
Close the book. Recite
the day’s Five to
remember. Only then
check.
3
Take the quiz
Each day ends with its
ten-question quiz. Note
every wrong answer.
4
Come back
Tick the tracker. Recall
the day again on day 3
and day 7, then use the
revision sheet.
Your revision tracker
Tick as you go. Day 3 and day 7 recalls are what move a fact from short-term to exam-day memory.

DAY  1  OF  26
1 October 2026
Thursday · 28 items · 6 topics
Agriculture 2  ·  Banking and Finance 4  ·  Reports & Articles 2  ·  Economy 6  ·  Social Issue 2  ·  Misc GA 12
NABARD  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
Agriculture
Southwest Monsoon 2026 Ends with 13% Rainfall Deficit
The 2026 southwest monsoon ended with rainfall at 87% of the Long Period Average (LPA).
India received about 759.4 mm of rainfall against the LPA of 868.6 mm.
The 13% deficit was the largest in more than a decade, according to the India Meteorological
Department (IMD).
A strong El Niño in the Pacific was one of the factors associated with the deficient monsoon.
Annual Survey of Industries 2024–25
The Annual Survey of Industries (ASI) 2024–25, released by MoSPI, reported 2.7 lakh factories and 1.67
crore workers.
The average wage per factory worker increased 5.3% to ₹2.28 lakh.
Jharkhand recorded a 5.2% fall in average wages, although its average wage of ₹3.04 lakh remained
above the national average.
Bihar recorded a 16.7% increase in average wages to ₹1.63 lakh.
RBI  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
Banking and Finance
SEBI Approves Reforms for Portfolio Management Services
SEBI approved changes to the Portfolio Management Services (PMS) regulatory framework.
PMS providers will be allowed to invest client funds in direct mutual fund schemes and Specialised
Investment Funds (SIFs) under the proposed framework.
PMS can also invest up to 10% of client AUM in investment-grade unlisted debt, subject to conditions
and client consent.
SEBI also approved changes to settlement proceedings, including formula-based settlement
amounts and a fast-track mechanism for certain violations.
India’s Net FDI Rises to Five-Year High
India's net FDI increased to US$7.35 billion in July 2026, the highest since May 2021.
Gross FDI inflows stood at US$14.58 billion during July.
Major sectors receiving equity inflows included communication, financial services and computer
services.

During April–July 2026, net FDI reached US$13.4 billion, compared with US$9.7 billion a year earlier.
India’s First Blue Bond by SMFCL
Sagarmala Finance Corporation Ltd. (SMFCL) launched India's first blue bond.
It aims to raise ₹600 crore, with a ₹500 crore green-shoe option.
The 10-year bond will support maritime lending, greenfield ports and coastal road projects.
Blue bonds are debt instruments used to finance projects linked to marine and water-related
sustainability.
IndusInd Bank Launches GCC Banking Vertical
IndusInd Bank launched a dedicated Global Capability Centre (GCC) Banking vertical.
It combines digital banking, employee banking, commercial cards, FEMA/capital-account solutions
and foreign-currency banking.
Its International Banking Unit at GIFT City will support cross-border banking requirements.
India's GCC ecosystem has grown significantly, with 2,117 GCCs.
RBI  AND  NABARD  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
Reports & Articles
1. Status of Policing in India Report (SPIR) 2026
Source: The Hindu
Context SPIR 2026 was released, based on a 16-state survey of 8,306 citizens — mapping cybercrime
exposure, vulnerability patterns and police redressal mechanisms.
What It Is
An empirical multi-state study of the everyday interface between citizens and the police
Tracking the lifecycle of cyber frauds — from digital exposure and victimisation to formal reporting
and financial recovery
Published jointly by Lokniti–CSDS and Common Cause
Key Findings
Cybercrime increased sharply: Cybercrime cases rose 17.9%, from 86,420 in 2023 to 1,01,928 in 2024,
while overall registered crime fell by about 6%.
High exposure to scams:
33% received calls about unordered deliveries.
31% encountered high-return investment scams.
27% received calls from fake bank officials seeking account details.
More internet use, more exposure: 46% of high internet users reported moderate-to-high scam
exposure, compared with 19% among non-internet users.
Cybercrime victims: Around 13% of respondents had directly experienced cybercrime in the previous
2–3 years. Among them, 54% faced financial fraud, and 23% lost more than ₹20,000.
Higher-income and educated groups also targeted: Financial fraud affected 57% of affluent victims
compared with 47% of poor victims. Among education groups, 59% of graduates were targeted
compared with 40% of non-literate citizens.
Low police reporting: Only 49% of victims filed a formal police complaint, while 39% did not complain

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 10 of 212
“Consistency beats intensity. Ask any topper.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
and 9% discontinued the process after the first visit.
Bribery and recovery: 27% of complainants reported paying a bribe. Among those who paid, 41%
recovered their full loss, compared with 12% among those who did not pay. This is a survey finding
showing an association, not proof that bribery causes better recovery.
Greater burden on poorer victims: 51% of poor victims reported paying a bribe, compared with 12% of
affluent victims. 55% of poor victims relied on informal influence networks to advance their cases.
Important Facts for Exams
Cybercrime: Crime committed using computers, mobile phones, networks or the internet.
Social Engineering: Manipulating people psychologically to obtain money, passwords or sensitive
information.
Cyber Fraud: Using digital technologies to deceive people and illegally obtain money or information.
CFCFRMS: System designed to coordinate rapid action to freeze funds lost through cyber financial
fraud.
I4C: Indian Cyber Crime Coordination Centre, under the Ministry of Home Affairs, which coordinates
India's cybercrime response.
CEIR: Central Equipment Identity Register, used to track and block lost or stolen mobile phones.
ASTR: Technology used to identify suspicious or fraudulent mobile connections.
Procedural Fatigue: Discouragement or withdrawal caused by lengthy, complicated or repeated
official procedures.
The Ageing World and India's Narrowing Window
Source: Business Standard
Context A new global demographics report by Moody's Ratings underscores a structural shift — more
than 70 per cent of the world's population now lives in countries with fertility at or below replacement
rate.
Key Highlights
Global ageing: Over 70% of the world’s population lives in countries with fertility at or below
replacement level. Global fertility fell from 4.9 (1950) to about 2.2.
India’s ageing: India entered the “ageing” category around 2024, with 65+ population crossing 7%. It
may become “aged” (14%) by 2049 and “super-aged” (21%) by 2065.
Demographic dividend: About 68% of Indians were aged 15–64 in 2025, but the working-age share is
expected to decline after 2030.
Economic opportunity: Demographic change contributed about 1.9 percentage points annually to
India’s growth during 1981–2021.
Women’s employment: Female labour-force participation is around 32%. Raising it to 50% could add
about 1 percentage point annually to GDP growth, according to the World Bank estimate cited.
Elderly population: Indians aged 60+ are projected to rise from 153 million (2022) to 347 million
(2050).
Policy challenge: India needs more jobs, skills and female employment while preparing for higher
demand for pensions, healthcare, geriatric care and senior housing.
Key Terms
Demographic Dividend: Economic growth potential created when the working-age population
becomes larger relative to dependants.
Replacement-Level Fertility: Fertility rate needed to maintain a broadly stable population; generally
around 2.1 children per woman.

Working-Age Population: Usually the population aged 15–64 years.
Ageing Population: A population in which the share of older people is increasing significantly.
Super-Aged Society: A society where 21% or more of the population is aged 65 or above.
Silver Economy: Economic activities and markets related to the needs of older people.
Female Labour-Force Participation Rate (FLFPR): Share of working-age women who are employed or
actively seeking employment.
Economy
US State Department Report on India’s Investment Climate
A US Department of State report assessed India's investment environment and highlighted issues
related to foreign-investment restrictions and regulatory practices.
The report noted that India permits 100% FDI in many sectors, while some sectors still require
government approval.
These include areas such as multi-brand retail, private banking, pharmaceuticals, defence, media
and satellites.
These are claims and assessments of the US report, rather than an independent conclusion.
CAFE-III Norms for Passenger Vehicles
The government notified the third phase of Corporate Average Fuel Economy (CAFE-III) norms.
The norms will apply from 1 April 2027 to 31 March 2032.
They tighten fleet-level fuel-efficiency requirements and provide incentives for EVs, hybrids, flex-fuel
vehicles and other cleaner technologies.
Under the framework, a battery electric vehicle counts as three vehicles for the fleet-efficiency
calculation.
Green Energy Corridor Phase III Approved
The Union Cabinet approved Green Energy Corridor Phase III with an outlay of over ₹1.86 lakh crore.
It aims to integrate up to 135 GW of renewable energy into the electricity grid by FY2032–33.
Around ₹1.36 lakh crore is allocated for intra-state transmission systems.
A dedicated ₹50,000 crore component will support 50 GWh of Battery Energy Storage Systems
(BESS).
India Participates in ASEAN-India Economic Ministers’ Meeting
India participated in the 23rd ASEAN-India Economic Ministers Consultation and the 14th East Asia
Summit–Economic Ministers’ Meeting in Manila.
Discussions covered trade, investment, energy security, industrial clusters and regional economic
cooperation.
India called for a balanced and trade-facilitative review of the ASEAN-India Trade in Goods
Agreement (AITIGA).
India-ASEAN trade reached US$128.38 billion in 2025–26.
HAL–SkyPulse ₹2,491 Crore Helicopter Programme
HAL and SkyPulse Solutions IFSC signed an MoU for a proposed ₹2,491 crore helicopter programme.
The programme covers 30 HAL-manufactured helicopters, with 10 planned during 2027–29 and 20

Applications include emergency medical services, disaster response, search and rescue, tourism

The programme also covers leasing, financing, MRO, training and engineering support.
India International Water Week 2026
India International Water Week 2026 was held at Bharat Mandapam, New Delhi, from 22–26
September.
The theme was “Climate Resilient Water Management.”
Participants from around 150 countries discussed sustainable and technology-driven water
management.
The event also promoted South-South cooperation and progress towards SDG 6 – Clean Water and
Sanitation.
Social Issue
Seva First Innovation Challenge 2026
The Seva First Innovation Challenge (SFIC) was launched to encourage young people to develop
solutions to community problems.
It has three levels: Junior, Technical and Open for school students, ITI/polytechnic students, and
higher-education/research participants.
Key themes include sustainable development, farmers, health, education and security.
The challenge is being organised by the Ministry of Education, AICTE and UGC, with IISc Bengaluru as
a nodal institution.
ECI’s Electoral Roll Management Systems
ECINET, launched by the Election Commission in 2026, is a unified digital platform integrating more
than 40 election-related portals and applications.
It provides services relating to electoral rolls, candidates, political parties and polling information.
The issue concerns the balance between centralised software systems and the statutory role of
Electoral Registration Officers (EROs).
The Maharashtra Chief Electoral Officer sought changes to preserve ERO discretion in handling voter-
data discrepancies.
Misc GA
Sunil Kumar Wins Bronze in Greco-Roman Wrestling
Sunil Kumar won bronze in the men's 87 kg Greco-Roman wrestling event.
He defeated the Philippines' Roberts Callum Johnston 9–0 by technical superiority.
He had earlier lost his semifinal 0–11 to Japan's Taizo Yoshida.
This became his second Asian Games medal, making him the first Indian Greco-Roman wrestler to
achieve this.
Lovlina Borgohain Reaches Asian Games Final
Lovlina Borgohain reached the women's 75 kg boxing final.

She became the first Indian woman boxer to reach back-to-back Asian Games finals.

India Reaches Women’s Hockey Final
India defeated South Korea 2–0 to reach the women's hockey final.
Ishika Chaudhary and Salima Tete scored for India.
India dominated possession but struggled to convert penalty corners during the early stages.
The result put India one step away from reclaiming the Asian Games women's hockey gold.
Sanskrit Subhashitam on Six Qualities of Success
The Sanskrit verse highlights six qualities: diligence, courage, patience, wisdom, strength and valour.
The verse conveys that human effort and ability are important for achieving success.
It links wisdom and courage with effective action.
The message combines the ideas of human effort and destiny.
Skytrax World Airline Awards 2026
Singapore Airlines was named the World’s Best Airline in the 2026 Skytrax World Airline Awards.
Qatar Airways ranked second and Cathay Pacific third.
Air India was the highest-ranked Indian airline at 57th globally.
IndiGo ranked 61st and was recognised as the Best Low-Cost Airline in India & South Asia.
TakeMe2Space to Launch Orbital Data Centre
Indian spacetech company TakeMe2Space plans to launch India's first networked orbital data-centre
mission in 2028.
Two satellites of about 100 kg each are planned for launch on a SpaceX Falcon 9.
Each satellite is planned to carry NVIDIA Thor GPUs, 100 TB storage and 1.5 kW power.
The project aims to enable in-orbit computing and data processing across satellites.
Bodhana Sivanandan Becomes Youngest Woman Grandmaster
Indian-origin British chess player Bodhana Sivanandan became the youngest Woman Grandmaster
(WGM) at age 11.
She secured her third and final WGM norm at the 46th Chess Olympiad.
She represented England at the Olympiad in Samarkand.
She also became one of the youngest players to achieve major international chess milestones.
V.S. Rathanvel Becomes India’s 99th Grandmaster
V.S. Rathanvel of Tamil Nadu became India's 99th Grandmaster.
He crossed the required 2,500 Elo rating.
The achievement came during the 3rd Guwahati Smart City International Open 2026.
The milestone added another Indian player to the country's growing pool of chess Grandmasters.
World Contraception Day 2026
World Contraception Day is observed on 26 September.

The 2026 theme was “No Barriers, Just Choices.”
It promotes awareness of contraceptive methods and informed reproductive-health choices.
The first observance was held in 2007.
International Day for the Total Elimination of Nuclear Weapons
Observed annually on 26 September.
It promotes awareness about the dangers of nuclear weapons and nuclear disarmament.
The UN General Assembly designated the day through Resolution A/RES/68/32 in 2013.
The first observance took place in 2014.
World Environmental Health Day 2026
Observed on 26 September every year.
The 2026 theme was “Environmental Health: Standing with Science to Protect Communities.”
It highlights the role of environmental health professionals in public health and community
protection.
The observance is coordinated by the International Federation of Environmental Health (IFEH).
Delhi Launches ‘Udyog Mitra’ Portal
Delhi launched the ‘Udyog Mitra’ portal under the Ease of Doing Business initiative.
It provides a single-window platform for business licences and NOCs.
The portal initially integrates 50+ services from 15 departments, with plans for further expansion.
It includes features such as Single Sign-On, Common Application Form, Know Your Approvals and
application tracking.

Schemes
National Coastal Mission 2.0
Source: ThePrint
Context The Union Environment Minister launched implementation guidelines for the National Coastal
Mission 2.0 (NCM 2.0) — a Central Sector Scheme with an outlay of ₹767 crore for 2025-26 to 2030-31,
across nine coastal States and four Union Territories.
What It Is
A Central Sector Scheme establishing an integrated ecological governance and marine spatial
planning framework
Subsuming NCM 1.0 (2021-22 to 2025-26)
To protect fragile coastal ecosystems and scale up nature-positive economic activities
Administered directly by the MoEFCC
Aim
Protect coastal and marine ecosystems
Strengthen climate resilience
Reduce marine pollution
Sustainably harness India's Blue Economy toward Viksit Bharat @2047
The Five Thematic Pillars
Blue Economy growth
Coastal risk reduction
Climate adaptation and mitigation
Capacity building
Effective project management
Important Facts for Exams
NCM 2.0 — ₹767 crore, 2025-26 to 2030-31; 9 coastal States and 4 UTs; administered by MoEFCC
ICZM — Integrated Coastal Zone Management; the ICZM Project has been World Bank-supported
since 2010
CRZ Notification, 2019 governs coastal regulation; zones CRZ-I to IV
NCSCM — National Centre for Sustainable Coastal Management, Chennai, the technical arm
Blue carbon — carbon stored by mangroves, seagrass and salt marshes; the Indian Sundarbans
stores about 24 million tonnes
India has a coastline of about 7,500 km; 40 per cent of mangroves lost over the past century
Green Climate Fund — the largest climate fund under the UNFCCC, established 2010

Air Ayodhya Receives NOC under UDAN
Air Ayodhya, operated by Megamax Aviation, received a NOC from the Ministry of Civil Aviation to
operate under RCS-UDAN.
It aims to connect Tier-2 and Tier-3 cities, pilgrimage centres and tourism hubs in Uttar Pradesh.
Operations are planned with Cessna Grand Caravan EX aircraft, later expanding to larger aircraft.
The airline plans to build a 50-aircraft fleet by 2036.
Reports & Articles
WHO-UNICEF Report on WASH in Schools
WHO and UNICEF released Progress on Drinking Water, Sanitation and Hygiene in Schools 2015–25
under the JMP-WASH programme.
Around 50 million Indian children lacked access to sanitation services at school in 2025, the highest
number globally.
Globally, 244 million children lacked school sanitation services.
About 20 million Indian children also lacked hygiene services at school.
Misc GA
UIDAI Launches Five New Aadhaar Initiatives
UIDAI launched five initiatives at Aadhaar Samvaad in Kolkata on 29 September 2026, marking the
17th Aadhaar Day.
Initiatives include Face Authentication for ePDS, SWIK Portal 2.0, Sub-AUA/Sub-KUA (LITE)
Framework, New Token Management System and a revamped UIDAI website.
Aadhaar Day commemorates the issuance of the first Aadhaar number on 29 September 2010.
MoMSME–MoSPI MoU for Statistical Business Register
MoMSME and MoSPI signed an MoU to strengthen the Statistical Business Register (SBR).
Udyam Registration data will be shared with MoSPI through APIs.
The data will help improve the coverage, consistency and reliability of business statistics.
SBR is a regularly updated database of businesses and economic units used for statistical purposes.
DRDO–CSIR-CGCRI Indigenous Bulletproof Glass
DRDO and CSIR-CGCRI are developing lightweight bulletproof glass for armoured vehicles.
The ₹50-crore, four-year project uses glass-ceramic technology and nanocrystals.
The project aims for multi-shot protection against AK-47-class threats.
Initial trials successfully stopped TAR rounds at around 695 m/s.

JNPA–IFC Blue Financing for Shore Power
Jawaharlal Nehru Port Authority partnered with IFC for India’s first blue financing for shore power
infrastructure.
The financing is worth USD 70 million (about ₹672 crore) through a 10-year ECB loan.
Shore Power Supply (SPS) allows ships at berth to use electricity from the grid instead of diesel
engines.
The project will help reduce fuel consumption and port-related emissions.
Apple Pay Launched in India
Apple Pay was launched in India with Axis Bank-issued Visa and Mastercard credit cards.
Transactions use tokenisation and can be authenticated through Face ID, Touch ID or a device
passcode.
The initial rollout covers sectors such as food and beverages, travel, fashion and lifestyle.
The initial service supports cards but not UPI or RuPay cards.
Bank of India Launches Programmable CBDC
Bank of India launched user-level programmable CBDC with auto-issuance.
Users can schedule recurring Digital Rupee (e₹) payments with fixed amounts and frequencies.
Users can also specify the purpose for which the recipient can use the funds.
Applications include allowances, education expenses and other recurring purpose-bound
payments.
RBI  AND  NABARD  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
1. The Green Energy Corridor Phase-III Scheme
Source: News on
Context The Union Cabinet approved the Green Energy Corridor Phase-III (GEC-III) scheme with an
outlay of ₹1,86,405 crore.
What It Is
A flagship central infrastructure scheme to expand and modernise state-level transmission grids
(InSTS)
Renewable Energy Evacuation: The scheme will enable evacuation of up to 135 GW of renewable
energy across States/UTs.
Battery Energy Storage: 50 GWh of Battery Energy Storage Systems (BESS) will be deployed to
improve grid flexibility.
Total Outlay:₹1,86,405 crore
₹1,36,378 crore – Intra-State Transmission Systems
₹50,000 crore – 50 GWh BESS
Central Financial Support:₹54,082 crore
This will help reduce intra-state transmission charges, ultimately keeping electricity costs lower.
Target: The projects are planned for completion by FY 2032-33.
Non-Fossil Energy Target: GEC-III will support India's goal of 900 GW installed non-fossil fuel
capacity by 2035.
Nodal Ministry: Ministry of New and Renewable Energy (MNRE)

Important Facts for Exams
Green Energy Corridor (GEC): Transmission network designed to integrate and evacuate electricity
generated from renewable energy sources.
Intra-State Transmission System (InSTS): Transmission network used to transfer electricity within a
State.
BESS: Battery-based system that stores electricity and supplies it when required.
TBCB: A competitive bidding system where transmission projects are awarded to the bidder offering
the required service at the most competitive tariff.
BOOM: Build-Own-Operate-Maintain model in which a private transmission provider builds, owns,
operates and maintains the infrastructure.
Brownfield Project: Expansion, upgrading or strengthening of an existing infrastructure/network.
Non-Fossil Capacity: Power capacity based on sources such as solar, wind, hydro and nuclear,
rather than fossil fuels.

Source: TH
Context The second edition of AIME commenced at Subic Bay, Philippines — with INS Sahyadri and INS

Key Details

The Sengol
Source: New Indian Express
Context The NCERT incorporated the Sengol into its newly released Class 9 Social Science textbook,
citing it as a historic symbol marking the transfer of power to Jawaharlal Nehru on the eve of
Independence in 1947.
What It Is
A ceremonial gold-plated silver sceptre rooted in ancient Tamil statecraft
Historically presented at the coronation of monarchs, as an emblem of righteous rulership, judicial
impartiality and adherence to dharma
Historical Background
Chola legacy — the tradition originated over a millennium ago under the Imperial Cholas, where the
high priest or royal guru handed the sceptre to a newly crowned sovereign, symbolising that
temporal authority is subordinate to ethical law
1947 — commissioned by the Thiruvaduthurai Adheenam, a centuries-old non-Brahmin Saivite mutt
in Tamil Nadu, and crafted by Madras jewellers Vummidi Bangaru Chetty; the five-foot sceptre was
presented to Jawaharlal Nehru in August 1947
Modern installation — preserved for decades at the Allahabad Museum, consecrated by pontiffs of
Tamil Nadu Adheenams, and installed beside the Lok Sabha Speaker's chair in the new Parliament
building in May 2023

Social Issue
International Day of the Deaf 2026
Observed on 27 September 2026, the last Sunday of September.
It promotes rights, inclusion, education and communication access for deaf people.
The World Federation of the Deaf (WFD) was established in 1951.
It is observed during the International Week of Deaf People.
International Day for Universal Access to Information 2026
Observed on 28 September to promote the right to access information.
2026 theme: “Upholding Information Integrity in the Digital Age: The role of access to Information in
addressing Information Disorder.”
UNESCO adopted the observance in 2015, while the UN General Assembly formally recognised it in 2019.
International Day of Awareness of Food Loss and Waste 2026
Observed on 29 September to highlight the need to reduce food loss and waste (FLW).
2026 theme: “Transformative Potential of Reducing Food Loss and Waste.”
The UN General Assembly established the day in 2019.
The first observance was held on 29 September 2020.

Lalit Kala Akademi National Exhibition of Art
The 65th National Exhibition of Art showcased 6,175 artworks by 2,495 artists.
The Lalit Kala Akademi announced 20 awards across visual art forms.
Major categories included painting, sculpture, drawing, printmaking and mixed media.
The exhibition was held at Rabindra Bhavan, New Delhi.
Edward Rukidi Kijanangoma Crowned King of Tooro
Edward Rukidi Kijanangoma was crowned the 13th King (Omukama) of Tooro Kingdom, Uganda.
He succeeded the late King Oyo Nyimba Kabamba Iguru Rukidi IV.
He was selected by a 21-member Babiito clan committee.
He adopted the regnal name Edward Rukidi Nyabongo I.

Schemes
The GOBARdhan Scheme
Source: TH
Context The Union Minister of Petroleum and Natural Gas launched the GOBARdhan Central Sector
Scheme with an outlay of ₹23,731 crore, to accelerate India's Compressed Biogas (CBG) sector over 10
years — FY2026-27 to FY2035-36.
What It Is
Objective: Promote domestic CBG production, reduce LNG imports and biomass burning, and
convert agricultural waste and cattle dung into green energy and bio-fertilisers.
Nodal Ministry:Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas
Capital Assistance: Up to ₹2 crore per TPD of installed CBG capacity for eligible greenfield plants.
Credit Guarantee: Up to 85% of outstanding defaults, with an exposure cap of ₹20 crore, increased to
₹25 crore for women-led enterprises.
Ecosystem Challenge Fund: Supports R&D, capacity building and district-level biomass mapping
through the Unified GOBARdhan Portal.
Aim
Boost domestic CBG production
Reduce LNG imports and biomass burning
Convert agricultural waste and cattle dung into green energy and bio-fertilisers
Important Facts for Exams
GOBARdhan: Galvanizing Organic Bio-Agro Resources Dhan — initiative for converting organic waste
into biogas, CBG and bio-fertilisers.
CBG: Purified biogas with high methane content that can be used as a CNG substitute.
FOM: Fermented Organic Manure, a useful by-product of CBG production.
TPD: Tonnes Per Day, a measure of plant production capacity.
SATAT: Sustainable Alternative Towards Affordable Transportation, launched in 2018 to promote
CBG production and its use as an alternative transport fuel.
India's First Female Farmer Worker Conference
Source: New Indian Express
Context India's first Female Farmer Worker Conference was held in New Delhi to recognise women's
contributions to agriculture and rural livelihoods — coinciding with the UN International Year of the
Woman Farmer 2026.

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 21 of 212
“The page you skip today is the question you miss tomorrow.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
What It Is
A national-level convention for celebrating, networking and addressing structural challenges faced
by women farmers, agricultural labourers and pastoralists
Organised by the Bharatiya Kisan Sangh (BKS)
Aim
Establish the formal social and legal identity of women as legitimate "farmers" (Kisan) rather than
unpaid family helpers
Facilitate access to institutional land rights and credit
Promote sustainable agrarian models
Important Facts for Exams
2026 is the UN International Year of the Woman Farmer (IYWF) — proposed by India and adopted by
the UNGA
The core issue is land title. Without a patta in her name, a woman cannot access institutional credit,
crop insurance, subsidies or compensation — regardless of how much farm work she does
Joint patta — land title recorded jointly in the names of husband and wife
Women constitute roughly one-third of the agricultural workforce and a far higher share of
operations in some activities, but own only about 14 per cent of operational holdings
Mahila Kisan Diwas is observed on 15 October, also the International Day of Rural Women
MKSP — Mahila Kisan Sashaktikaran Pariyojana, under DAY-NRLM
Female LFPR: 41.7 per cent (2023-24) on usual status; agriculture absorbs the largest share
India ranks 131st on the Global Gender Gap Index 2026
PRAGATI-50 Initiative for Clean Cities
MoHUA launched PRAGATI-50 under SBM-U 2.0 to develop 50 clean role-model cities across 24
States.
It will use KPI-based monitoring and technology-enabled governance.
A Swachh Bharat Test Bed & Innovation Centre will be established at Durgapur with CSIR-CMERI.
A new cohort of sanitation and waste-management startups was also launched with IIT Kanpur.
National Coastal Mission 2.0
NCM 2.0 aims to strengthen conservation and sustainable management of India's coastal and marine
ecosystems.
It has a total budget of ₹767 crore and will continue up to 2030–31.
It covers 9 coastal States and 4 Union Territories.
Focus areas include climate-resilient blue economy, coastal risk reduction, climate adaptation and
capacity building.

Banking and Finance
Small Savings Scheme Interest Rates Unchanged
The Ministry of Finance kept Small Savings Scheme (SSS) interest rates unchanged for Q3 FY27
(October–December 2026).
Rates have now remained unchanged for 14 consecutive quarters.
The last revision was made in Q4 FY24.
SSS instruments are government-backed savings products administered mainly through Post Offices
and designated banks.
ICICI Lombard Launches VIBE Health Insurance
ICICI Lombard launched VIBE, a health insurance product aimed at Gen Z customers aged 18–35
years.
It combines hospitalisation, outpatient care, mental-health support, fitness benefits and wellness
rewards.
It offers six variants, with coverage from ₹7 lakh to ₹1 crore.
The product uses the company's IL TakeCare digital ecosystem.
Pramerica Life Launches Poorna Rakshak
Pramerica Life Insurance launched Poorna Rakshak, a non-linked, non-participating individual
savings life insurance plan.
It offers five variants based on different financial and income needs.
Variants include Smart Scholars, Early Income, Income Booster, Smart Money Back and Smart Saver.
The plan combines life protection with guaranteed benefits.
Jio Financial Services–Allianz Capital Infusion
Jio Financial Services and Allianz Europe invested a combined ₹640.10 crore in their 50:50 Jio Allianz
General Insurance joint venture.
Each partner invested ₹320.05 crore through a rights issue.
The capital will support the expansion of general insurance operations in India.

CAG-BHASHINI Anuvaad
Source: PIB
Context The Digital India BHASHINI Division (DIBD) and the Comptroller and Auditor General of India
launched CAG-BHASHINI Anuvaad — an AI-powered multilingual translation solution for official and
audit-related documents.
What It Is
An AI-enabled multilingual document translation solution
Developed by BHASHINI specifically for the CAG
To translate official documents and audit reports into Indian languages
Aim
Integrate Language AI into CAG workflows
Enabling faster and consistent multilingual translation
While ensuring human oversight, security and data sovereignty
Important Facts for Exams
CAG-BHASHINI Anuvaad — by the Digital India BHASHINI Division and the CAG
BHASHINI supports 36 Indian text languages, 23 Indian voice languages, 35 international languages
BHASHINI — Bhasha Interface for India, launched 2022 under the National Language Translation
Mission; an initiative of MeitY
It is built as Digital Public Infrastructure for language, with an open API ecosystem
CAG — Article 148; appointed by the President; tenure six years or age 65
The CAG audits accounts under Articles 149-151; reports go to the President or Governor, who lays
them before Parliament or the State Legislature
CAG reports are examined by the Public Accounts Committee, the Committee on Public
Undertakings and the Estimates Committee
Dr B. R. Ambedkar called the CAG the "most important officer under the Constitution"
India has 22 scheduled languages in the Eighth Schedule; Hindi and English are the languages of the
Union under Articles 343-351

The Cabinet approved the Intelligent Traffic Management System (ITMS) for Delhi at an estimated
cost of ₹1,789.52 crore.
The project will cover 42 traffic corridors.
It will be implemented in three phases over 24 months, followed by five years of operation and
maintenance.
The project aims to improve traffic management, urban mobility and transport infrastructure.

transmission networks.
It will facilitate the evacuation of up to 135 GW of renewable energy.
The project supports India's expansion of renewable-energy infrastructure.

Important Days
World Rabies Day – 28 September
Observed annually on 28 September to promote rabies prevention and elimination.
2026 theme: “Stronger Together.”
The day was established in 2007 by the Global Alliance for Rabies Control.
It commemorates the death anniversary of Louis Pasteur, developer of the first successful rabies
vaccine.
World Rivers Day – 27 September
Observed on the fourth Sunday of September to promote river conservation and sustainable
management.
2026: 27 September.
The initiative was associated with Canadian river conservationist Mark Angelo.
The first World Rivers Day was observed in 2005.
World Heart Day – 29 September
Observed to raise awareness about cardiovascular diseases (CVDs) and heart health.
2026 theme: “Don’t Miss a Beat.”
The first World Heart Day was observed in 2000.
The observance is associated with the World Heart Federation and WHO.
International Translation Day – 30 September
Observed on 30 September to recognise the role of language professionals in international
communication.
2026 theme: “Linguistic Diversity and Language Rights.”
The UN officially recognised the day through UNGA Resolution A/RES/71/288 in 2017.
The date coincides with the feast day of St. Jerome, regarded as the patron saint of translators.

4 & 5 October 2026
30 items · 6 topics
Agriculture 2  ·  PIB 2  ·  Banking and Finance 9  ·  Economy 3  ·  Schemes 2  ·  Misc GA 12
NABARD  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
Agriculture
Tiger Reintroduction at Buxa Tiger Reserve
A three-year-old tigress (T138) was released into Buxa Tiger Reserve, West Bengal, starting a phased
tiger reintroduction programme.
The tigress was translocated from Valmiki Tiger Reserve, Bihar.
Three more tigers are planned to be released over the next two months.
The aim is to establish a viable and self-sustaining tiger population in Buxa.
ICAR–FCI MoU on Alternative Fumigation Technologies
ICAR and FCI signed an MoU to develop safer and more sustainable alternatives to chemical
fumigation for wheat and rice storage.
Phase-I has an approved outlay of ₹3.28 crore and will be implemented by ICAR-CIPHET.
Technologies being tested include nitrogen, carbon dioxide and combined CO₂-phosphine
fumigation.
The programme aims to reduce worker, environmental and ecological risks associated with
conventional fumigation.
PIB
ICAR–FCI MoU on Alternative Fumigation Technologies
ICAR and Food Corporation of India (FCI) signed an MoU to develop alternative fumigation
technologies for wheat and rice storage.
The two-year Phase-I will be implemented by ICAR-CIPHET, Ludhiana.
It seeks alternatives to aluminium phosphide and malathion, whose repeated use has contributed to
pest resistance and health risks.
Pest resistance in key Indian states has reached 72–100%, increasing fumigation costs and risks to
workers and the environment.
National Biodiversity Authority – 23rd Foundation Day
The National Biodiversity Authority (NBA) observed its 23rd Foundation Day.
The focus was on strengthening Access and Benefit Sharing (ABS).
ABS ensures that benefits arising from the use of biological resources and associated traditional
knowledge reach local communities.

NPS Swasthya
Source: Mint
Context The PFRDA launched NPS Swasthya on 1 October 2026, observed as NPS Diwas — pairing a

the only pension fund manager to launch the scheme.
How It Works
Pairs a dedicated investment corpus with a super top-up health insurance policy
Aims to help individuals prepare for health contingencies without disrupting long-term retirement
savings
A family floater covering the subscriber's spouse and children — parents are excluded
Sum insured: ₹1 lakh to ₹30 lakh
The first-year premium is paid upfront; from the second year, renewal premiums can be funded from
the NPS Swasthya balance
Important Facts for Exams
NPS Swasthya launched 1 October 2026 (NPS Diwas) by PFRDA; first offered by Axis Pension Fund
Sum insured ₹1 lakh to ₹30 lakh; family floater excluding parents; 12-month pre-existing disease
waiting period; renewal only to age 85
Withdrawal: up to 25 per cent of own contributions, no waiting period or frequency limit
Deductibles: ₹10,000 / ₹50,000 / ₹1 lakh / ₹3 lakh for covers of ₹1 lakh / ₹5 lakh / ₹10 lakh / ₹30 lakh
NPS Vatsalya — for minors; Atal Pension Yojana — for the unorganised sector

Rising Child Leprosy Cases Challenge India's 2027 Goal
Source: The Hindu
Context India recorded 3,832 new child leprosy cases in 2025-26, including 37 with Grade-2 disability at
diagnosis — 91.6 per cent above the National Leprosy Eradication Programme's target of 2,000 cases.
The National Figures (2025-26)
Indicator Figure
Total new leprosy cases 91,783
New child cases 3,832
Child cases with Grade-2 disability 37
Total G2D cases at diagnosis 1,945 — 2.12 per cent of new cases
Prevalence rate 0.56 per 10,000 population
National G2D rate 1.34 per million — above the target of under one per million
India's Global Position
Per the WHO's latest global update, for 2025:
Country New cases
India 91,783
Brazil 22,901
Indonesia 16,292
The three together account for 78.3 per cent of all new cases globally
India also reported the world's largest number of new child cases — 3,832
India's data covers April 2025 to March 2026, though presented in the WHO's 2025 table
High-Burden States
Districts with prevalence above one per 10,000:
Chhattisgarh — 23 districts
Jharkhand — 21
Maharashtra — 18
Odisha — 18
Madhya Pradesh — 10
About Leprosy
Also called Hansen's disease
A chronic bacterial infection, caused mainly by Mycobacterium leprae
Primarily affects the skin and peripheral nerves
Transmitted mainly through prolonged, close contact with an untreated person
Curable with multidrug therapy (MDT)

RoDTEP Scheme Extended to 31 December 2026
Source: News on Air
Context The Department of Commerce extended the Remission of Duties and Taxes on Exported
Products (RoDTEP) Scheme up to 31 December 2026.
What It Is
A WTO-compliant export promotion and tax neutrality scheme
Refunding previously un-rebated Central, State and local levies, taxes and duties borne on exported
goods
Including prior-stage cumulative indirect taxes on inputs and transport
Came into effect on 1 January 2021, replacing the WTO-disputed Merchandise Exports from India
Scheme (MEIS)
Who Runs It
Notified by: the DGFT, Department of Commerce, Ministry of Commerce and Industry
Administered and implemented by: the CBIC, Department of Revenue, Ministry of Finance
Aim
Neutralise embedded domestic taxes on outbound goods
Eliminate cost disabilities for domestic producers
Prevent the export of domestic taxes
Enhance the cost competitiveness of Indian merchandise globally

Desert National Park
Source: IE
Context The Union Environment Minister released two captive-bred male Great Indian Bustards into the

What It Is

Location

A hyper-arid zone receiving under 100 mm annual rainfall

of the Wildlife (Protection) Act; state bird of Rajasthan; locally Godawan

RBI Grants CIC Registration to Indiabulls

(CIC).
A CIC is an NBFC mainly engaged in holding investments in and providing loans to group companies.
Generally, at least 90% of net assets of a CIC must be in group-company investments/loans, with at
least 60% in equity investments.
The registration brings Indiabulls under the RBI regulatory framework for CICs.
RBI  &  NABARD  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine 2026
Source: Indian Express
Context
The 2026 Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine was jointly awarded to Karl Deisseroth, Peter Hegemann
and Georg Nagel for discoveries that led to optogenetics — a technique that uses light to control
specific nerve cells.
Key Highlights
What is Optogenetics?
It is a technique that uses light-sensitive proteins to control the activity of specific neurons.
Scientists can switch selected brain cells ON or OFF using light.
Role of Channelrhodopsin
Peter Hegemann and Georg Nagel discovered channelrhodopsin, a light-sensitive protein in the
single-celled alga Chlamydomonas.
When exposed to blue light, this protein opens a channel and allows ions to flow into the cell,
producing an electrical signal.

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 37 of 212
“Rest is part of the plan, not a break from it.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Contribution of Karl Deisseroth
Deisseroth introduced the gene for channelrhodopsin into nerve cells.
In 2005, he demonstrated that light could trigger nerve signals.
In 2007, the technique was successfully used to control nerve cells in the living brain of mice.
Why is it important?
Earlier, scientists could mainly observe brain activity.
Optogenetics allows them to actively control specific neurons and establish a cause-and-effect
relationship between brain circuits and behaviour.
It has helped researchers study memory, pain, emotions, thirst, hunger, reward, attention and
social behaviour.
Medical Applications
It is being studied for neurological and psychiatric conditions such as Parkinson's disease,
Alzheimer's disease, epilepsy, depression and schizophrenia.
Researchers are also exploring it to restore vision in people with certain forms of blindness.
Example
Think of the brain as a huge electrical circuit. Earlier, scientists could watch the circuit working, but
could not easily control one particular wire.
Optogenetics = putting a light-controlled switch on selected neurons.
So, scientists can shine light on a particular group of neurons and observe what happens to memory,
movement, emotion or behaviour.
Key Terms
Optogenetics: Use of light to control genetically modified cells, especially neurons.
Channelrhodopsin: A light-sensitive protein that acts as a switch for controlling cell activity.
Light-gated ion channel: A protein channel that opens or closes when exposed to specific light.
Neuron: A nerve cell that transmits electrical and chemical signals.
Neural circuit: A network of interconnected neurons responsible for a particular function or behaviour.
Causal relationship: Showing that one event directly produces a particular effect.

The Ramappa Temple
Source: TH
What It Is
A 13th-century Hindu temple dedicated to Shiva as Ramalingeswara or Rudreshwara
Inscribed as a UNESCO World Heritage Site in July 2021 — India's 39th — as "Kakatiya Rudreshwara
(Ramappa) Temple, Telangana"
At Palampet village, Venkatapur Mandal, Mulugu district
History
Built in 1213 CE under Ganapati Deva, by his general Recharla Rudra
Construction took roughly 40 years
Named after its chief sculptor Ramappa — perhaps the only historic temple in India named after its
builder rather than the deity
Important Facts for Exams
Built 1213 CE; UNESCO listed July 2021; India's 39th World Heritage Site
Ganapati Deva (Kakatiya ruler); general Recharla Rudra; sculptor Ramappa
Sandbox technique gives earthquake resistance; floating bricks reduce structural load
Madanika — a sculpted female figure; Yali — a mythical composite beast in South Indian temple art
Kakatiyas ruled from Warangal, 12th–14th centuries; Rani Rudrama Devi was among India's few
women rulers
3D-Printed Rural Houses under PMAY-G
Source: The Hindu
Context Andhra Pradesh will launch India's first national-level pilot to construct rural houses using 3D
Concrete Printing (3DCP) under PMAY-Gramin.
What 3DCP Is
An automated, construction-scale additive manufacturing technology
Replacing traditional brick-and-mortar masonry by depositing high-performance cementitious
mixtures layer by layer, using robotic gantry or arm printers
How It Works
Digital modelling — a 3D plan created using Building Information Modelling (BIM), sliced into precise
numerical computer paths
Material extrusion — a computer-guided robotic nozzle extrudes specially engineered fluid concrete
along the toolpath
Rapid in-situ setting — the mix maintains high buildability and fast setting, forming load-bearing
walls without conventional formwork

The engine is being developed for the Ghatak Unmanned Combat Aerial Vehicle (UCAV).
It recorded a maximum dry thrust of 48.5 kN, exceeding the target of 46 kN.
The engine is a non-afterburning version of the Kaveri engine.
India’s First Free-Space Quantum Key Distribution Link
QNu Labs, BISAG-N and IIT Gandhinagar demonstrated India’s first free-space Quantum Key
Distribution (QKD) link.
The secure communication link covered 5.56 km.
It achieved a Quantum Bit Error Rate below 5% and generated secure keys at 230–260 bps.
QKD uses quantum principles to enable highly secure encryption-key distribution.
New Lizard Species Discovered in Peru
Scientists discovered a new cryptic lizard species named Liolaemus arequipensis in southern Peru.
It was found at an altitude of about 4,818 metres in the Arequipa region.
It belongs to the Liolaemus genus, a group of South American lizards mainly found in the Andes.
The species was described in the scientific journal Diversity.
Asian Games 2026 – India Finishes Fourth
The Aichi–Nagoya Asian Games 2026 concluded on 4 October 2026.
India finished 4th with 85 medals: 21 gold, 27 silver and 37 bronze.
China topped the table with 341 medals, followed by Japan and South Korea.
The Games featured 469 medal events across 43 sports.
Filmmaker Singeetham Srinivasa Rao Passes Away
Veteran filmmaker Singeetham Srinivasa Rao passed away at the age of 94.
He directed around 60 films across several Indian languages.
Notable works include Pushpaka Vimanam, Aditya 369, Mayuri and Bhairava Dweepam.
He received 2 National Film Awards and several state and Filmfare awards.

A global assessment of land degradation
With practical strategies for scaling up high-quality Forest and Landscape Restoration (FLR)

That is 10 per cent of the world's total land area
And about 5.2 times the size of India

RBI  AND  NABARD  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
2026 Nobel Prize in Physics: Neutrinos and the IceCube Observatory
Source: Indian Express
Context of the News
The 2026 Nobel Prize in Physics was awarded to Francis Halzen, a Belgian-American scientist, for his
pioneering work on detecting high-energy neutrinos using the IceCube Neutrino Observatory at the
South Pole in Antarctica.

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 44 of 212
“Every day you show up, the syllabus gets smaller.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Key Highlights
What are Neutrinos?
Neutrinos are extremely tiny subatomic particles produced mainly during nuclear reactions, such as
those occurring inside the Sun.
They are among the most abundant particles in the universe, after photons.
They are electrically neutral and interact very weakly with matter, earning them the nickname “ghost
particles.”
Billions of neutrinos pass through our bodies every second without noticeable effects.
Why are Neutrinos Important?
Because neutrinos interact very weakly with matter, they can travel through very dense regions of
space without being absorbed or scattered.
This allows scientists to study cosmic events that cannot be observed clearly through ordinary light.
Neutrinos therefore provide a new window into the universe.
What did Francis Halzen do?
Francis Halzen developed the idea of using the deep Antarctic ice itself as a huge neutrino detector.
This led to the development of the IceCube Neutrino Observatory at the South Pole.
IceCube covers about 1 cubic kilometre of ice and contains more than 5,000 light sensors installed
deep inside the Antarctic ice.
How does IceCube detect neutrinos?
A neutrino occasionally collides with an atomic nucleus in the ice.1.
The collision produces a charged particle.2.
The charged particle travels through the ice and produces a faint blue light/glow called Cherenkov3.
light.
IceCube's light sensors detect this glow.4.
Scientists use the direction and energy of the light to determine the direction and energy of the5.
original neutrino.
Why Antarctica?
The South Pole provides a large volume of extremely clear and stable ice.
Deep ice is naturally dark and isolated, reducing interference from other sources.
This makes it suitable for detecting the extremely rare interactions of neutrinos.
India's Rising Supercomputing Ecosystem
Source: PIB
Context of the News
India has deployed 40 indigenous supercomputers with a combined capacity of 68 Petaflops under the
National Supercomputing Mission (NSM) — strengthening self-reliance in high-performance
computing (HPC) ahead of ESTIC-2026.
Key Highlights
What Is the National Supercomputing Mission?
Launched in April 2015 with an outlay of ₹4,500 crore
Jointly run by the Department of Science and Technology (DST) and the Ministry of Electronics and
Information Technology (MeitY)

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 45 of 212
“Every day you show up, the syllabus gets smaller.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Implemented by C-DAC, Pune and IISc, Bengaluru
Its aim is to build an indigenous high-performance computing system, so India does not depend on
other countries for computing power
How Did Indian Supercomputing Develop?
PARAM 8000 (1991) — India's first indigenous supercomputer, built by C-DAC, with a speed of 1
Gigaflop. It was made after Western countries refused to sell India the Cray X-MP machine
PARAM Yuva series (2008–2013) — raised Indian computing to the Teraflop level (54 TF), used for fluid
dynamics, weather modelling and bioinformatics
NSM launched (2015) — shifted from building one-off machines to a connected national grid
PARAM Shivay and Pravega (2019–2022) — the first multi-petaflop systems. PARAM Shivay at IIT BHU
was the first built under NSM, followed by PARAM Pravega (3.3 PF) at IISc Bengaluru
PARAM Rudra (2024 onwards) — uses Indian-designed Rudra servers, Indian high-speed
interconnects and Indian liquid-cooling parts

The National Statistics Office (NSO) released the findings of India's first-ever National Household Travel

What Is the Survey?
India's first all-India household travel survey
It records why people travel, how they travel, how far, for how long and how much they spend
Covered 4.92 lakh households and over 20.34 lakh persons
Conducted by the NSO under MoSPI
Important note: in this survey, "travel" means a trip of 1 km or more. Shorter trips are not counted
Important Facts for Exams
NHTS — India's first household travel survey; July 2025 to June 2026; 4.92 lakh households, 20.34
lakh persons; by the NSO under MoSPI
"Travel" means trips of 1 km or more
Students: 66.4 per cent travel 1 km+; walking 37, bus 29.5, bicycle 15.8 per cent
Workers: 57.6 per cent travel 1 km+; urban 64.1, rural 54.5 per cent
Two-wheelers: 42.6 per cent overall; 52.3 urban, 37.1 rural
Gender gap: 61.9 per cent men against 41.9 per cent women
Average travel time: 23 minutes (students), 25 minutes (workers)
Monthly commute cost: ₹785 average; ₹1,044 urban, ₹612 rural

The female work-travel gap connects to India's low female labour force participation of 41.7 per cent
(PLFS 2023-24)
Pneumonic Plague
Source: TOI

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 46 of 212
“Every day you show up, the syllabus gets smaller.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Context of the News
Nearly 200 people were quarantined and monitored in Irkutsk, Siberia, after a researcher at an anti-
plague institute died of severe pneumonia suspected to be pneumonic plague.
Key Highlights
What Is Pneumonic Plague?
The rarest, most severe and most deadly form of plague
It is an infection of the lungs
Caused by the bacterium Yersinia pestis
It is the only form of plague that spreads directly from person to person
Important Facts for Exams
Pneumonic plague — the deadliest form and the only one spreading person to person
Caused by Yersinia pestis; carried by rodents, spread by the rat flea (Xenopsylla cheopis)
Incubation: 24 hours to 3 days
Three forms: bubonic (lymph nodes), septicaemic (blood), pneumonic (lungs)
Treated with gentamicin, doxycycline, ciprofloxacin
Yersinia pestis is named after Alexandre Yersin, who identified it in Hong Kong, 1894
Plague caused three historic pandemics — the Plague of Justinian (6th century), the Black Death
(14th century) and the Third Pandemic (19th century), which reached Bombay in 1896
India's last major outbreak was in Surat, 1994
The Haffkine Institute, Mumbai was founded after the 1896 plague; Waldemar Haffkine developed
the first plague vaccine there
India-Japan Joint Crediting Mechanism (JCM)
Source: PIB
Context of the News
India and Japan jointly launched the Operational Manual for the India-Japan Joint Crediting
Mechanism (JCM) in New Delhi — moving the arrangement from design to actual project execution.
Key Highlights
What Is the JCM?
A bilateral project-based mechanism started by Japan with partner developing countries
Japan supplies advanced low-carbon technology, infrastructure and services
The emission reductions achieved are counted as credits and shared between the two countries
What Is the Legal Basis?
Governed by Article 6.2 of the Paris Agreement, which allows two countries to cooperate voluntarily
and transfer emission reductions between them
These transferred reductions are called ITMOs — Internationally Transferred Mitigation Outcomes
Built on a Memorandum of Cooperation signed 7 August 2025
And Rules of Implementation adopted 8 June 2026

Agriculture
Up to 30% of Cotton Area Under Illegal GM Hybrids
Source: Indian Express
Context of the News
A recent field-based study by the Federation of Seed Industry of India (FSII) and National Seed
Association of India (NSAI) estimates that 24–30% of India’s cotton area may be under unauthorised
GM cotton hybrids containing traits not approved by the government.
Key Highlights
Scale of cultivation: Of 114.8 lakh hectares under cotton in 2025–26, around 26.2–29 lakh hectares
may involve unauthorised GM hybrids.
Genes legally allowed: India currently permits GM cotton with two Bt genes — cry1Ac and cry2Ab.
These provide protection against certain bollworm pests.
Unauthorised genes found:
cp4-epsps: Provides tolerance to glyphosate herbicide, allowing farmers to spray glyphosate for
weed control without killing the cotton plant.
Vip3A: A Bt-derived gene offering protection against a broader range of insect pests, including pink
bollworm.
Study coverage: Researchers tested 227 geo-tagged seed/leaf samples from fields of 984 farmers
across 16 districts in Maharashtra, Telangana, Andhra Pradesh and Rajasthan.
HT gene prevalence: The cp4-epsps gene was detected in 72.69% of field samples.
Telangana: 88.64%
Maharashtra: 70.92%
Andhra Pradesh: 62.86%
Rajasthan: 57.14%
Vip3A prevalence: 53.74% of samples tested positive for the Vip3A gene.
Why farmers are adopting it: Labour shortages and high manual weeding costs are major drivers.
Glyphosate-based weed control may cost around ₹750–850/acre, compared with
₹12,000–15,000/acre for manual weeding.
Illegal seed market: Unauthorised HTBt cotton seed sales are estimated to have increased from 35
lakh packets in 2017–18 to 82–88 lakh packets in 2025–26, potentially worth ₹820–880 crore.
Glyphosate imports: Imports increased by about 29%, from 27,721 kilolitres in 2024–25 to 35,642
kilolitres in 2025–26.
Key Terms
GM Crop: A crop whose genetic material is modified using biotechnology to introduce a desired trait.

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 51 of 212
“Small steps, every day. That is the whole strategy.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Bt Cotton: Cotton genetically modified with genes from Bacillus thuringiensis to protect against insect
pests.
HT Cotton: Herbicide-Tolerant Cotton, designed to survive application of a particular herbicide.
cp4-epsps: A gene that gives plants tolerance to glyphosate.
Vip3A: A Bt-derived insect-resistance gene effective against several cotton pests.
Biosafety Regulation: Government rules governing the development, testing, approval and release of
genetically modified organisms.
Pink Bollworm: A major cotton pest that damages cotton bolls and reduces yield and fibre quality.
WWF Living Planet Report 2026
Source: Indian Express
Context of the News
The WWF's biennial Living Planet Report 2026 found a 73 per cent average decline in the sizes of
monitored vertebrate populations — mammals, fish, birds and amphibians — over the 52 years from 1970
to 2022.
Key Highlights
73% decline: Average monitored vertebrate population sizes declined by 73% between 1970 and 2022,
based on data from 35,803 populations and 5,790 species.
Freshwater ecosystems: Recorded the highest decline at 85%, followed by terrestrial ecosystems
(69%) and marine ecosystems (59%).
Regional decline: The largest declines occurred in Latin America & Caribbean (95%), Africa (80%),
and Asia-Pacific (49%).
Major drivers: Habitat loss and degradation, overexploitation, climate change, invasive species,
pollution and disease.
Food system: The way food is produced and consumed is identified as the single largest driver of
biodiversity loss, contributing to habitat destruction, overharvesting and greenhouse-gas emissions.
Recovery is possible: Green turtles, bluefin tuna, tigers and southern African elephants have shown
recovery due to targeted conservation efforts. Global wild tiger numbers increased from around 3,200
in 2010 to 5,700 in 2025.
Living Planet Index (LPI)
LPI measures the average rate of change in population size of monitored vertebrate species across
terrestrial, freshwater and marine ecosystems. It tracks relative changes in population abundance, not
the total number of animals lost.
Key Terms
Biodiversity: Variety of living organisms, ecosystems and genetic resources.
Living Planet Index: Indicator tracking changes in monitored vertebrate populations.
Vertebrates: Animals with a backbone, such as mammals, birds, fish, reptiles and amphibians.
CBD: Global agreement for biodiversity conservation, sustainable use and benefit sharing.
Cartagena Protocol: International agreement dealing with biosafety and GMOs.
Nagoya Protocol: Agreement governing access to genetic resources and fair sharing of benefits.
Important Facts for Exams
WWF — founded 1961, headquartered at Gland, Switzerland; its symbol is the giant panda

“Small steps, every day. That is the whole strategy.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Rural Economy Becoming More Resilient to Monsoon Shocks
An RBI study found that India's rural economy is becoming less dependent on rainfall because of non-
farm activities, allied sectors and better irrigation.
For small farm households owning up to one acre, wages contribute more than 55% of income, while
crops and livestock together contribute about 36%.
Even when rainfall is highly deficient, non-agricultural rural growth remains relatively strong, while
agricultural growth falls sharply.
The finding is important for understanding rural income diversification and climate resilience.
VB–G RAM G Act: Permissible Rural Works Expanded to 375
The Ministry of Rural Development expanded permissible works under the Viksit Bharat – Guarantee
for Rozgar and Ajeevika Mission (Gramin) [VB–G RAM G] Act, 2025 from 266 to 375, a 41% increase.
The works are grouped under Water Security, Core Rural Infrastructure, Livelihood Infrastructure
and Disaster Preparedness.
181 works are agriculture-related, including water management, land development, plantations,
livestock and productive assets.
Works will be planned through Viksit Gram Panchayat Plans, approved by the Gram Sabha.

Banking and Finance
RBI Raises Repo Rate to 5.5% and Shifts to Calibrated Tightening
Source: ET
Context of the News
The Monetary Policy Committee (MPC) raised the policy repo rate by 25 basis points to 5.5 per cent
and shifted its stance from neutral to calibrated tightening — signalling that rate cuts are off the table
in the near term.
Key Highlights
What Are the New Rates?
Rate New level
Repo rate 5.50 per cent
Standing Deposit Facility (SDF) 5.25 per cent
Marginal Standing Facility (MSF) 5.75 per cent
Bank Rate 5.75 per cent
Why Did RBI Raise the Repo Rate?
The main reason is that the inflation outlook has become less comfortable, while economic growth
remains strong.
Crude oil prices: Oil prices have risen above $100 per barrel, creating inflationary pressure because
India is heavily dependent on imported crude.
Geopolitical tensions: The escalation of the West Asia conflict has increased uncertainty and oil-
price volatility.
Food inflation: Weak monsoon conditions and El Niño-related risks could put pressure on food prices.

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 53 of 212
“Small steps, every day. That is the whole strategy.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Rupee weakness: A weaker rupee makes imports, particularly crude oil, more expensive.
Strong growth: India's economy remains strong, giving the RBI greater room to raise borrowing costs.
What Does “Calibrated Tightening” Mean?
Calibrated tightening means the RBI is gradually moving towards a tighter monetary policy to
control inflation.
Inflation risk ↑ → Repo Rate ↑ → Borrowing becomes costlier → Demand may moderate → Inflation
pressure may reduce
Impact on Loans
The repo-rate hike is likely to make floating-rate loans more expensive.
Home loans: EMIs may increase or loan tenure may rise.
Auto loans: New borrowing may become costlier.
MSME loans: Cost of credit may increase.
Consumer demand: Higher EMIs can reduce demand for interest-sensitive goods and services.
Deposits: Banks may gradually increase deposit rates to attract funds.
Key Terms
Repo Rate: Rate at which RBI lends short-term money to commercial banks against eligible securities.
Basis Point (bps): 1 bps = 0.01 percentage point; therefore, 25 bps = 0.25 percentage point.
MPC: Six-member body responsible for deciding the policy repo rate.
Monetary Policy Stance: RBI's broad approach towards interest rates and liquidity.
Calibrated Tightening: A gradual move towards higher interest rates to contain inflation.
SDF: Facility through which banks deposit surplus funds with RBI without collateral.
MSF: Overnight borrowing facility available to eligible banks from RBI against specified securities.
CPI Inflation: Inflation measured using changes in the Consumer Price Index.

The campaign is supported by market institutions including NISM, BSE, NSE, MCX, CDSL, NSDL and
AMFI.
RBI  &  NABARD  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
2026 Chemistry Nobel: Mirror-Image Molecules
Source: Indian Express
Context of the News
The 2026 Nobel Prize in Chemistry was awarded to Henri Kagan (France) and Kenso Soai (Japan) for
showing how homochirality — nature's preference for one mirror-image form of a molecule — can be
created in the laboratory.

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 54 of 212
“Small steps, every day. That is the whole strategy.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Key Highlights
What Is Chirality?
Some molecules exist in two forms, each the mirror image of the other — like your left and right
hands
The two forms have the same atoms, connected in the same order
The only difference is the three-dimensional arrangement
One cannot be placed exactly on top of the other — they are non-superimposable
The two forms are called enantiomers
Why Does It Matter in Biology?
The amino acids in proteins and the sugars in DNA occur in only one form
The mirror versions exist but life rarely uses them
This preference is called homochirality
What Was the Laboratory Problem?
Ordinary chemical reactions produce both enantiomers in roughly equal amounts
So homochirality was long thought to be unique to living things
What Did Each Scientist Contribute?
Scientist Period Contribution
Willy Marckwald Early 1900s First reaction giving slightly unequal amounts, using a chiral catalyst
Henri Kagan 1980s Showed that a mildly chiral catalyst can produce strongly asymmetric results —
disproving the assumed linear relationship
Kenso Soai 1990s Created the first asymmetric autocatalytic reaction, where the product acts as its
own catalyst, yielding 99.5 per cent of one enantiomer
Why Does This Matter for Medicines?
Many drug molecules are chiral
The body responds to only one enantiomer; the other may be useless or harmful
Earlier: companies made both forms, then separated the useful one — expensive and wasteful
Now: the desired form can be made directly, through asymmetric synthesis
Also applies to flavours, fragrances and pesticides
The Integrated Transport and Logistics Authority (ITLA)
Source: PIB
Context of the News
The Union Cabinet approved setting up a dedicated Special Purpose Vehicle (SPV) — the Integrated
Transport and Logistics Authority (ITLA) — as the national apex body for transport and logistics
planning.
What Is ITLA?
A national apex institution, set up as a Special Purpose Vehicle
To institutionalise integrated, multimodal transport planning, big-data analytics, project technical
appraisal and policy support across all freight and transit areas
Modelled on similar agencies in Japan, the United States, South Korea, China and Australia

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 55 of 212
“Small steps, every day. That is the whole strategy.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Why Was It Needed?
India's transport infrastructure has historically suffered from fragmented planning
Road, rail, aviation and shipping ministries worked in isolation from one another
PM GatiShakti (2021) and the National Logistics Policy (2022) improved coordination
But a dedicated mechanism was still missing for three things: technical appraisal, transport data
management and multimodal project monitoring
Key Terms
SPV (Special Purpose Vehicle): A separate legal entity created for one defined purpose — a company,
not a body created by an Act of Parliament
Multimodal transport: Moving goods using more than one mode, such as road to rail to port
Rolling plan: A plan revised and extended regularly, so it always looks a fixed number of years ahead
Technical appraisal: Checking whether a project is sound in design and engineering; financial
appraisal checks whether it makes money sense
Origin-destination analysis: Studying where goods begin and end their journeys, to locate where
capacity is short
Vahan and SARATHI: The national databases for vehicle registrations and driving licences
Silo: A department working in isolation, without sharing information or coordinating with others

Herugavialis cairensis: Gharial Relative Found in Egypt
Source: TOI
Context of the News
Palaeontologists discovered Herugavialis cairensis, a new extinct crocodilian species, in 45-million-
year-old limestone of Egypt’s Mokattam Formation — the same rock formation associated with the
Great Sphinx and Giza pyramids.
What Is It?
An extinct semi-aquatic crocodilian of the gharial superfamily (Gavialoidea)
Originally classified in 1927 as Tomistoma cairense
Advanced 3D cranial modelling revealed unique internal skeletal features, warranting an entirely new
genus
How Was It Named?
Heru (Horus) — the Egyptian deity associated with the Great Sphinx; chosen because the skull’s
missing snout tip resembles the Sphinx’s missing nose
cairensis — after Cairo
When and Where Did It Live?
Middle Eocene Epoch, about 45 million years ago, during a global greenhouse climate phase
In warm, shallow coastal waters and lagoons of the ancient Neo-Tethys Sea, which once covered
northern Egypt and the Mediterranean basin

accounting for about two-thirds of the national population.

2026 Nobel Prize in Literature: Anne Carson
Source: TH
Context of the News
The 2026 Nobel Prize in Literature was awarded to Anne Carson, a Canadian poet, classicist, essayist
and translator.
About Anne Carson
Nationality: Canadian
Born: 21 June 1950, Toronto, Canada
Profession: Poet, essayist, translator, classicist and professor
Field of expertise: Ancient Greek literature and classical studies
Achievement: The 19th woman to receive the Nobel Prize in Literature. She is also the second Canadian
recipient, after Alice Munro, who won in 2013.
Why Did She Win?
The Swedish Academy honoured Carson for her bold and inventive body of work, which uses
classical literature to create new forms of contemporary writing.
Her work combines poetry, prose, translation and literary criticism. She often uses ancient Greek
myths and stories to explore modern themes such as love, identity, loss and grief.
Important Works
Autobiography of Red (1998): A novel in verse that reimagines the ancient Greek myth of Geryon.
Eros the Bittersweet (1986): A work exploring love and desire through ancient Greek poetry.
The Beauty of the Husband (2001): A fictional essay in 29 sections called tangos, exploring love and
marriage.
Nox (2010): An experimental book about the death of her elder brother, combining poetry,
photographs and personal materials.
If Not, Winter (2002): An English translation of the surviving fragments of ancient Greek poet Sappho.

Scientific agriculture and improved seeds

A Pre-Rabi Kisan Goshti-cum-Agricultural Input Distribution Programme

To enable direct interaction between farmers, scientists and policymakers
Key Terms
Integrated farming: Combining crops, livestock, fisheries and horticulture on the same farm, so the
waste of one becomes input for another
Balanced nutrient management: Applying nitrogen, phosphorus and potassium in correct proportion,
rather than over-using urea
Agro-climatic conditions: The combination of soil, rainfall and temperature that determines what can
be grown in a place
SCSP and TSP: Sub-plans requiring a share of scheme funds to be earmarked for Scheduled Caste
and Scheduled Tribe beneficiaries
Kisan Goshti: A farmers' meeting where scientists and officials interact directly with farmers

Important Facts for Exams
Birsa Munda — the tribal freedom fighter and leader of the Ulgulan rebellion; 15 November, his birth

National Strategy for Additive Manufacturing (NSAM) 2.0
The Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology (MeitY) is rolling out NSAM 2.0, building on the
first strategy launched in 2022.
Additive manufacturing is a process of creating objects layer by layer using 3D model data. Unlike
subtractive manufacturing, which removes material, it adds material to build the object.
Key Steps: The process involves 3D design using Computer-Aided Design (CAD), converting the design
into a digital file, slicing it into layers, depositing or fusing materials, and post-processing.
Applications: It can be used in aerospace, defence, healthcare, automobiles and advanced
manufacturing, enabling complex designs and reducing material waste.

Banking and Finance
RBI Raises Daily CRR Maintenance Requirement
Source: ET
Context of the News
The RBI said banks must maintain 99 per cent of the required Cash Reserve Ratio daily from 16 October,
up from 90 per cent at present — to reduce surplus liquidity in the banking system.
Key Highlights
CRR rate remains unchanged at 3%: Banks must continue to maintain 3% of their Net Demand and
Time Liabilities (NDTL) as cash reserves with the RBI.
Daily maintenance rises from 90% to 99%: Banks must now maintain at least 99% of their prescribed
CRR on every day of the reporting fortnight, compared with 90% earlier.
Purpose: The RBI is trying to absorb excess liquidity and bring short-term market interest rates closer
to the policy repo rate.
Banking system liquidity surplus: It was around ₹3.88 lakh crore on 8 October 2026.
Understanding the CRR Change with an Example
Suppose a bank has an NDTL of ₹1,000 crore.
Particulars Amount
Prescribed CRR rate 3%
Total CRR required ₹30 crore
Earlier minimum daily maintenance (90%) ₹27 crore

CRR vs SLR
Basis CRR SLR
Full form Cash Reserve Ratio Statutory Liquidity Ratio
Maintained with/in RBI Banks themselves
Form Cash reserves Cash, gold and eligible securities
Interest/return No interest on CRR balances Eligible securities may earn returns
Main purpose Manage liquidity and monetary
conditions
Maintain liquidity and support financial stability
Key Terms for Exams
CRR (Cash Reserve Ratio): The percentage of a bank's NDTL that must be maintained as cash reserves
with the RBI. Banks do not earn interest on these reserves.
SLR (Statutory Liquidity Ratio): The percentage of NDTL banks must maintain in specified liquid assets,
such as cash, gold and eligible government securities.
NDTL (Net Demand and Time Liabilities): A bank's net demand and time liabilities, broadly
representing deposits and certain other liabilities used to calculate reserve requirements.
Repo Rate: The rate at which the RBI lends money to banks against eligible securities.
Liquidity: The availability of money and funds in the banking system.
OMO (Open Market Operations): The RBI's purchase or sale of government securities to manage
liquidity. Selling securities generally absorbs liquidity.
WACR (Weighted Average Call Rate): The weighted average interest rate on overnight unsecured
funds borrowed and lent in the interbank call money market.

World Development Report 2026
Source: PIB
Context of the News
The World Bank Group's World Development Report 2026: The Promise of Artificial Intelligence was
launched in New Delhi by the Union Minister for Electronics and IT, jointly hosted by MeitY's IndiaAI
Mission.
About the World Development Report (WDR) 2026
Published by: World Bank Group.
Nature: The World Bank Group’s annual flagship report on important development policy issues.
Focus: Examines how Artificial Intelligence (AI) can support economic development, affect jobs and
improve public services, especially in low- and middle-income countries.
Key Highlights of the Report
Adopt and Adapt Strategy: Developing countries should customise existing AI models for local needs
instead of building costly models from scratch.
Job Displacement: Just over 4% of Indian jobs face immediate automation risks, versus over one-third
in advanced economies. Entry-level BPO job postings in South Asia fell by around 20%.
Fiscal Risks: Tax incentives, including a reported 20-year tax holiday for foreign data centres, may
strain public finances and raise electricity costs.
AI Sovereignty: Domestic data centres cannot ensure technological independence due to
dependence on imported semiconductors, cloud infrastructure and global supply chains.
AI Talent Drain: India lost a net 200 elite AI researchers and inventors (2011–2024) despite having
around 50,460 AI professionals in 2025.
AI Preparedness: Only 1 of 25 low-income economies had an active national AI strategy.
Public Perception: 18.8% of Indians, compared with 50.5% of Americans, expressed concern about AI.

Banking and Finance
Foreign Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR)
Source: IE
Context of the News
The RBI introduced the Foreign Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) — a measure new to Indian markets,
borrowed from China — as part of a package to defend the rupee, which had fallen close to its all-time
low.
What Is FERR?
Banks must set aside 20 per cent of any forex derivative contract above $2 million involving the
rupee
The amount is kept in cash with the RBI
The RBI can change the percentage as conditions require
It earns no return for the bank
What Does It Actually Do?
It makes protection against a fall in the rupee more expensive
Banks must lock up cash against every such contract, so they price the hedge higher or offer fewer of
them
Why Discourage Hedging?
This is the point that needs careful understanding:
Hedging is normally good practice — importers protect themselves from a falling rupee
But to hedge, you must buy dollars forward
That adds to dollar demand today
Which pushes the rupee down further
So when a currency is already under pressure, hedging activity deepens the fall
FERR raises the cost of that activity, slowing it
How Is This the China Model?
China has long used a Foreign Exchange Risk Reserve Ratio of 20 per cent
In March 2026, China cut its ratio to zero — because the yuan was strengthening, and it wanted to
remove the cost of betting against its own currency
India has done the reverse, introducing the ratio because the rupee is weakening
Key Terms
Forex derivative: A contract whose value depends on a future exchange rate — a forward, future,
option or swap
Hedging: Buying protection against an adverse future price or rate movement
Underlying: The actual exposure a derivative is meant to protect — such as an import bill
Rebooking: Cancelling a hedge and taking a fresh one at a better rate; often used to speculate rather
than protect
Speculative demand: Buying a currency to profit from its movement, not because it is needed for a
transaction
Structural demand: Regular, unavoidable demand — such as oil companies needing dollars every
month
