Indo-French TRISHNA Satellite to Launch in 12–16 Months
TRISHNA stands for Thermal Infra-Red Imaging Satellite for High-resolution Natural Resource
Assessment.
Partnership: It is a joint satellite mission of the Indian Space Research Organisation (ISRO) and France’s
National Centre for Space Studies (CNES).
Purpose: To monitor land surface temperature, water stress, vegetation health and energy exchanges
using thermal infrared observations.
Importance: The data will support agriculture, water-resource management, climate studies and
environmental monitoring.
India Records the World’s Largest Share of Demonstrations
Source: The Armed Conflict Location & Event Data Project (ACLED), a United States-based organisation
tracking political violence and demonstrations.
Number of Demonstrations: India recorded 18,074 demonstrations through September 2026,
accounting for 17.5% of the global total.
Trend: The number increased from 11,020 in 2017. India reportedly recorded the largest share globally
for five consecutive years.
Other Countries: Pakistan, Mexico and France were among the other countries in the top five, each
accounting for approximately 4–5%.

Scientists Report the First Working Nuclear Clocks
Development: Scientists from Europe and China reported the first working nuclear clocks in two papers
published in the journal Nature.
Difference: Atomic clocks measure time using transitions in an atom’s electrons, while nuclear clocks
use transitions within an atom’s nucleus.
Importance: Nuclear clocks could offer highly precise time measurement and help scientists study
fundamental forces and possible changes in physical constants.
Applications: Potential uses include advanced navigation, precision measurement and fundamental
physics research.

NABARD  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
Agriculture
The Sarus Crane
Source: Down To Earth
Context of the News
The Bombay Natural History Society (BNHS) announced a nationwide All-India Sarus Crane Survey —
to estimate the population baseline, map breeding and roosting strongholds, and assess collision
threats to India's tallest flying bird.
What Is It?
The world's tallest flying bird
India's only resident breeding crane species
In Indian culture, an enduring symbol of marital fidelity and lifelong companionship
State Bird of Uttar Pradesh
IUCN: Vulnerable
Important Facts for Exams
Sarus Crane (Antigone antigone) — the world's tallest flying bird, up to 1.5 m tall, wingspan 2.4 m
IUCN: Vulnerable; Schedule I of the Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972; CITES Appendix II
State Bird of Uttar Pradesh; India's only resident breeding crane
Survey announced by the BNHS — founded 1883, headquartered at Mumbai
India holds the largest global population, concentrated in Uttar Pradesh, especially the Etawah-
Mainpuri belt; also in Gujarat, Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh and Maharashtra
Electrocution on power lines is the leading cause of death — the same threat facing the Great Indian
Bustard
The main habitat is farmland, so protection depends on wetland conservation outside protected
areas
Keoladeo National Park, Bharatpur — a UNESCO World Heritage Site and Ramsar Site
World Bank Backs Kerala’s Carbon Credit Cell Proposal Under KERA Project
The World Bank backed Kerala’s proposal to establish a Carbon Credit Cell under the Department of
Agriculture as part of the ₹2,365 crore Kerala Climate Resilient Agri-Value Chain Modernisation (KERA)
Project.
The initiative aims to promote climate-friendly farming, increase carbon sequestration and provide
additional income opportunities for farmers.
KERA is a five-year project covering 2024–2029, focused on climate-resilient agriculture and stronger
agricultural value chains.

Nagaland Launches SAY-SOBLOK to Support 4,000 Vulnerable Households
The Nagaland State Rural Livelihoods Mission partnered with BRAC International to launch SAY-SOBLOK,
a three-year initiative targeting 4,000 extremely vulnerable households.
The initiative operates under Samaveshi Aajeevika Yojana (SAY) and the Deendayal Antyodaya
Yojana–National Rural Livelihoods Mission (DAY-NRLM).
Support includes livelihood development, financial inclusion, household coaching and access to

Sex Reversal in Animals Raises Concerns About Extinction Risk
A study published in Science Advances examined wild agile frogs (Rana dalmatina) in Hungary.
Sex reversal occurs when an animal develops reproductive traits that differ from its genetically
determined sex.
The study found that climate change-related sex reversal can create reproductive disadvantages and
increase the risk of extinction.
Lakshadweep Achieves 100% Literacy
Lakshadweep was declared a fully literate Union Territory under the ULLAS–Nav Bharat Saaksharta
Karyakram.
A survey identified 1,113 adult non-literate people across the islands, who received literacy and
numeracy training.
The learners successfully passed the Foundational Literacy and Numeracy Assessment Test.
RBI  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
Banking and Finance
SEBI's Credit Risk-o-Meter for Debt Securities
Source: News on Air
Context of the News
SEBI issued a circular introducing a mandatory colour-coded Credit Risk-o-Meter, to help investors
assess the credit risk of debt securities.
What Is It?
A visual, colour-coded indicator showing how risky a debt security is
Based on mapping existing credit ratings to simple risk levels
How Does It Work?
SEBI's existing credit ratings, from AAA to D, are mapped to six risk levels
Ranging from "Lowest Credit Risk" to "High to Very High Risk of Default"
Each level has its own colour
Where Must It Be Displayed?
Offer documents
Abridged prospectuses
Private placement memoranda
All advertisements by issuers and Online Bond Platform Providers (OBPPs)

The web and mobile platforms of OBPPs
Which Instruments Are Covered?
Non-Convertible Securities (NCS)
Commercial Papers (CPs)
Securitised Debt Instruments (SDIs)
Security Receipts (SRs)
Structured Debt and Market-Linked Debentures (MLDs)
Key Terms
Credit risk: The risk that the borrower will fail to repay
Credit rating: An agency's assessment of that risk, from AAA (safest) down to D (default)
NCD / NCS: Non-Convertible Debenture or Security — a bond that cannot be converted into shares
Commercial Paper: A short-term unsecured borrowing instrument used by companies
MLD: Market-Linked Debenture — a bond whose return depends on the performance of a market index
Private placement: Selling securities to a selected group rather than the public
OBPP: Online Bond Platform Provider — a SEBI-registered platform selling bonds to retail investors

2026 Nobel Prize in Economics
Source: IE
Context of the News
American economists Robert E. Hall and Michael Woodford were awarded the 2026 Nobel Prize in
Economic Sciences — for providing an empirical formula to help governments frame stabilisation
policy, incorporating household demand behaviour and firms' supply behaviour.
Who Are the Winners?
Name Age Position
Robert E. Hall 83 Robert and Carole McNeil Joint Hoover Senior Fellow and Professor of Economics,
Stanford University
Michael Woodford 71 John Bates Clark Professor of Political Economy, Columbia University
What Is Stabilisation Policy?
Measures taken by the government and central bank to reduce major swings in growth, inflation and
employment.
Fiscal policy — the government changes spending and taxes. Example: raising infrastructure
spending during a slowdown to create jobs
Monetary policy — the central bank changes interest rates to influence borrowing, spending and

inflation. Example: cutting rates to encourage investment
The idea originates with John Maynard Keynes, whose work gained importance during the Great
Depression (1929-1939). He argued that government intervention may be needed when private
spending is too weak.
World Bank: India's Poverty Rate to Fall to 12.1% by 2028
Source: Business Standard
Context of the News
The World Bank's October 2026 Poverty and Equity Brief projects India's poverty rate at the $4.2-a-day
threshold to fall from 14.2 per cent in 2026 to 12.1 per cent by 2028 — though the pace of reduction is
expected to moderate.
Key Highlights
Decline in Poverty in India
Poverty rate: Projected to decline from 14.2% in 2026 to 12.1% by 2028.
Lower-middle-income poverty: Fell from 57.7% in 2011 to 16.1% in 2023, lifting an estimated 496 million
people out of poverty.
Extreme poverty: Declined from 27.1% in 2011 to 2.6% in 2023.
Higher poverty threshold: Around 80% of India's population remained below the upper-middle-
income poverty line of $8.30 per person per day in 2023.
World Bank Poverty Lines
Poverty category Daily poverty line
Extreme poverty $3.00
Lower-middle-income $4.20
Upper-middle-income $8.30
Factors Behind Poverty Reduction
Rising Consumption: Improved household spending on essential goods and services.
Employment Growth: Around 134 million additional people employed between 2017 and 2023.
Welfare Support: Food subsidies and social transfers support low-income households.
E-commerce Growth: Online shopping and quick-commerce services create jobs.
Rural–Urban Poverty Gap
Area (2023) Poverty rate
Rural India 19.3%
Urban India 8.6%

Agriculture
Nameri Wild Classroom
Source: TH
Context of the News
The Assam Forest and Climate Change Department launched the Nameri Wild Classroom at Nameri
Tiger Reserve — a programme that takes students into the forest itself for hands-on learning.
What Is It?
A residential environmental education programme
Students attend two- and three-day field camps inside the tiger reserve
The point is to move learning out of textbooks and into a living habitat
What Does It Aim to Do?
Build curiosity and scientific temper among young people
Give direct exposure to forest and river ecosystems
Create future conservationists and researchers
Important Facts for Exams
Nameri National Park lies in Sonitpur district, Assam, on the Assam-Arunachal Pradesh border
It became a Tiger Reserve in 1999-2000
The Jia Bhoroli river (called the Kameng in Arunachal) flows along its boundary
It adjoins Pakke Tiger Reserve in Arunachal Pradesh, forming a continuous habitat
Famous for the White-winged Wood Duck — Assam's state bird, listed as Endangered on the IUCN
Red List
Assam's other tiger reserves: Kaziranga, Manas and Orang
Kaziranga and Manas are UNESCO World Heritage Sites
OPEC Cuts India’s 2026 Oil Demand Growth Forecast to 40,000 Barrels per Day
The Organisation of the Petroleum Exporting Countries (OPEC) reduced its forecast for India’s oil
demand growth in 2026 to 40,000 barrels per day (bpd), down from 60,000 bpd in its September
report.
India’s total oil demand is projected to reach 5.7 million barrels per day in 2026.
Petrol and diesel are expected to drive demand, supported by vehicle sales, road transport,
manufacturing and agricultural activities.
Demand for liquefied petroleum gas (LPG) and naphtha is expected to decline.

RBI  AND  NABARD  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
BIMSTEC Delhi Declaration 2026
Source: The Hindu
Context of the News
India hosted the first-ever meeting of BIMSTEC Home Ministers in New Delhi. The bloc adopted the Delhi
Declaration, aimed at countering radicalisation, terror financing and the misuse of new technologies
by terrorist groups.
What Is the Meeting?
The first ministerial-level gathering of Home Ministers from the seven BIMSTEC member states
Held in New Delhi, hosted by India
Its purpose is to give strategic direction to regional security cooperation
Background
Year Event
2018 The 4th BIMSTEC Summit at Kathmandu proposed Home Ministers’ Meetings, alongside the existing
National Security Advisors’ mechanism
2025 The 6th BIMSTEC Summit at Bangkok welcomed the operationalisation of the Home Ministers’ Meeting
mechanism
2026 India hosted the inaugural meeting in New Delhi
What Is BIMSTEC?
Full form: Bay of Bengal Initiative for Multi-Sectoral Technical and Economic Cooperation
Seven members: Bangladesh, Bhutan, India, Myanmar, Nepal, Sri Lanka, Thailand
Founded in June 1997 as BIST-EC — Bangladesh, India, Sri Lanka, Thailand
Myanmar joined later that year, making it BIMST-EC
Nepal and Bhutan joined in 2004, after which it took its present name
Established by the Bangkok Declaration, 1997
Secretariat: Dhaka, Bangladesh, set up in 2014
The BIMSTEC Charter came into force in 2024, giving the grouping a formal legal identity

PM GatiShakti Completes Five Years
Source: News on Air

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 92 of 212
“Keep the streak. Momentum is the cheapest fuel there is.”

Context of the News
The PM GatiShakti National Master Plan completed five years on 13 October 2026. Around the same
time, the Union Cabinet approved the Integrated Transport and Logistics Authority (ITLA), which will
appraise infrastructure projects worth ₹500 crore or more.
What Is PM GatiShakti?
India's digital planning framework for infrastructure
It combines GIS mapping, satellite imagery and databases from different ministries on one platform
The idea is to move away from each department planning on its own, toward a whole-of-
government approach
Key Highlights
Launched on: 13 October 2021
Launched by: Prime Minister Narendra Modi
Full name: PM GatiShakti National Master Plan for Multi-Modal Connectivity
Nodal ministry: Ministry of Commerce and Industry, through the Department for Promotion of Industry
and Internal Trade (DPIIT).
Main objective: Integrated infrastructure planning and better coordination among ministries and
states.
Technology: Geographic Information System (GIS), satellite imagery and digital mapping.
Developed by: BISAG-N (Bhaskaracharya National Institute for Space Applications and
Geoinformatics).

The International Monetary Fund (IMF) raised India’s growth forecast for FY2026–27 to 7%, from 6.4%
estimated in July.

Oman Replaces the United States as India’s Largest LNG Supplier
Oman became India’s largest supplier of liquefied natural gas (LNG) in September 2026, replacing the
United States.
India imported 2.17 million tonnes of LNG during the month, 14% less than in the previous comparison
period.
LNG is natural gas cooled into liquid form to make storage and transportation easier.

10th National Convention of Agrivision 2026
Source: PIB
Context of the News
The 10th National Convention of Agrivision 2026 was held in New Delhi on 13-14 October. The theme
running through it was India's shift from production-centric agriculture to market-oriented agri-food
value chains.
What Is Agrivision?
A national agricultural convention
It brings together students, scientists, policymakers, entrepreneurs and farmers
The focus areas are innovation, agricultural education, entrepreneurship and farmer-centric
development
Who Organised It?
Agrivision, under the ICAR Agricultural Education Division
Venue: the ICAR-NASC Complex, Pusa, New Delhi
What Is the Aim?
Strengthen collaboration among agricultural stakeholders
Promote market-driven agriculture
Improve farmer profitability through innovation and value addition
Soybean Varieties NRC 268 and NRC 270
Source: PIB
Context of the News
At a Soybean Harvest Day organised by ICAR-National Soybean Research Institute, Indore, two new
varieties — NRC 268 and NRC 270 — showed preliminary yields of about 20 quintals per hectare.
What Are They?
High-yielding, climate-resilient soybean (Glycine max) varieties
Developed by ICAR-NSRI, Indore, Madhya Pradesh
Meant to raise farm productivity, seed quality and returns for oilseed growers
What Are Their Key Traits?
Climate and disease resilience — better tolerance of irregular rainfall, dry spells, pests and diseases
NRC 270 is bold-seeded — larger grains, higher seed weight, better market value
High oil and protein content — supporting both edible oil extraction and protein-rich soybean meal
for livestock feed

Jeypore Ground Gecko
Source: The Hindu
Context of the News
Only four individuals of the endangered Jeypore ground gecko have been recorded in the past year in
Odisha's Balda hills — inside the site of a proposed bauxite mining project.
What Is the Jeypore Ground Gecko?
A nocturnal lizard, about 3.6 inches long
Comparatively large head, yellow-orange body, strikingly patterned
Lives in rock crevices and under leaf litter
Found in the Eastern Ghats of Odisha and Andhra Pradesh
Important Facts for Exams
Feature Details
Scientific name Cyrtodactylus jeyporensis
Family Gekkonidae
Distribution Eastern Ghats of Odisha and Andhra Pradesh
IUCN status Endangered (EN)
CITES status Appendix II
Rediscovered 2010, after more than a century without recorded sightings
Major threats Bauxite mining, deforestation, forest fires and habitat loss
Indian Army Launches Bharat Rice for Defence Forces
The Indian Army launched Bharat Rice to supply quality rice to Army Supply Depots, including remote
areas of Ladakh and the Northeast.
The Directorate General of Supplies and Transport (DGST) is leading the initiative.
The National Agricultural Cooperative Marketing Federation of India (NAFED) will procure and supply
rice in 10 kg and 30 kg bags during Financial Year 2026–27.
The Army signed a five-year, extendable Memorandum of Understanding with the Food Corporation of
India (FCI) to use its infrastructure, equipment and trained manpower during operational
contingencies.

Banking and Finance
SEBI to Expand AIF Framework
Source: The Hindu
Context of the News
SEBI will soon issue a circular expanding the framework for Alternative Investment Funds (AIFs). The
change is expected to raise the pool of accredited investors from 1 lakh to about 4 lakh.

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 99 of 212
“A fact revised three times is a mark in the bank.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
What Are AIFs?
Privately pooled investment vehicles
They raise money from institutional investors and high-net-worth individuals
They invest in alternative asset classes — unlisted securities and other non-traditional investments
Because they hold complex and often illiquid assets, they carry higher risk
So they follow a separate regulatory framework from retail products such as mutual funds
Important Facts for Exams
AIFs are regulated under the SEBI (Alternative Investment Funds) Regulations, 2012
Three categories:
Category I — venture capital, SME, social venture and infrastructure funds; government-
encouraged sectors
Category II — private equity and debt funds; no special incentives and no leverage
Category III — hedge funds and funds using complex trading strategies; may use leverage
Minimum investment in an AIF: ₹1 crore, reduced to ₹25 lakh for employees and directors of the fund
Minimum corpus: ₹20 crore, or ₹10 crore for an angel fund
RBI  AND  NABARD  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
Khajuraho's Javari Temple
Source: Times of India
Context of the News
The Archaeological Survey of India announced that the missing head of the 11th-century Vishnu idol at
Khajuraho's Javari Temple has been traced in an ASI store — ending a dispute that had reached the
Supreme Court.
What Is the Javari Temple?
A compact, finely sculpted Hindu temple dedicated to Vishnu
Part of the Khajuraho Group of Monuments, a UNESCO World Heritage Site since 1986
Located in the Eastern Group of Temples, Chhatarpur district, Madhya Pradesh
Important Facts for Exams
Javari Temple — dedicated to Vishnu; in the Eastern Group, Khajuraho, Chhatarpur district, Madhya
Pradesh
Built 1075-1100 CE under the Chandela dynasty
Khajuraho Group of Monuments — UNESCO World Heritage Site, 1986
Of about 85 original temples, around 25 survive, in three groups — Western, Eastern and Southern
The Western Group is the largest and includes the Kandariya Mahadeva Temple, the biggest and
most ornate
Khajuraho temples follow the Nagara style — curvilinear shikhara, no boundary wall, built on a raised
jagati
They are built of sandstone, mostly without mortar, using interlocking stone
Lakshadweep Declared 100% Literate Under ULLAS
Source: News on Air

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 100 of 212
“A fact revised three times is a mark in the bank.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Context of the News
Lakshadweep was declared a 100 per cent literate Union Territory by Administrator Praful Patel, under
the ULLAS-Nav Bharat Saaksharta Karyakram.
What Is ULLAS?
Full form: Understanding Lifelong Learning for All in Society
Also called the Nav Bharat Saaksharta Karyakram or New India Literacy Programme (NILP)
A Centrally Sponsored Scheme
Aligned with the National Education Policy 2020
Targets adults aged 15 and above who missed formal schooling
Who Runs It and For How Long?
Department of School Education and Literacy (DoSEL), Ministry of Education
A five-year cycle, from FY2022 to FY2027
Vision: "Jan Jan Saakshar"
What Does "100 Per Cent Literate" Mean?
Under ULLAS, a place is declared fully literate when it crosses 95 per cent literacy among the target
population, not a literal 100 per cent.
The standard is set by the Ministry of Education, and the measurement is through the Foundational
Literacy and Numeracy Assessment Test (FLNAT).

Wholesale Price Inflation Rises to 11.4% in September
India's Wholesale Price Index (WPI) inflation rose to 11.4% in September, a 49-month high according to
the longer historical series mentioned in the report.
Inflation in food articles increased to 8.1%, compared with 5.7% in the previous month.
Inflation in non-food primary articles stood at 10.3%.
WPI measures changes in the prices of goods at the wholesale level, before they reach consumers.
India–China Trade: Exports Rise 40.7%, Trade Deficit Crosses $100 Billion
During January–September 2026, India's exports to China increased 40.7% to $19.26 billion.
Imports from China reached $125.19 billion, an increase of 23.3%.
Total two-way trade reached $144.45 billion, growing by 25.4%.
India's trade deficit with China reached $105.93 billion, meaning India imported substantially more
from China than it exported.

grow by 4%.

grow by around 4% in 2026.

Internet Shutdowns in India
India's share of global internet shutdowns reportedly increased from 37% in 2016 to 63% in 2020, before
declining to 21% in 2025.
Despite this decline, India ranked second globally in 2025, behind Myanmar and ahead of Pakistan.
Protests accounted for 47% of shutdown cases in 2025, compared with 8% in 2021, according to the
figures in the supplied article.
Internet shutdowns restrict access to online communication and services and raise concerns about
access to information, livelihoods and public safety.
Social Issue
The Myth of “Unskilled” Labour in India
A The Hindu article argues that classifying workers as “unskilled” can affect their wages, workplace
safety and job security.
Safe in India Foundation data cited in the article for the automotive sector in 2024 showed an average
of 2.27 fingers lost per accident among the lower-paid “unskilled” category, compared with 1.54
among the “skilled” category.
The article links this difference to the assignment of dangerous machinery without adequate training
and safety measures.
Uttar Pradesh announced an interim monthly wage increase to ₹13,690 for “unskilled” workers and
₹16,868 for “skilled” workers.
India's First Household Travel Survey
The National Statistics Office conducted India's first all-India household travel survey between July
2025 and July 2026.
The survey covered more than 14,000 villages and 10,000 urban blocks.
Two-wheelers accounted for 43% of workers' travel, while public transport accounted for 15% and cars
for 3%.
Average commuting time was 25 minutes; average monthly spending on travel to a fixed workplace
was ₹785. The survey also reported a gap in daily work trips between male workers (62%) and female
workers (42%).

NABARD s
State of the World's Forests 2026
Source: FAO
Context of the News
The FAO released the State of the World's Forests (SOFO) 2026. The report says the world needs to
restore 470 million more hectares by 2030 to meet existing pledges. India ranks first in the world in forest
area actively under restoration.
What Is SOFO?
The FAO's flagship forest report, published every two years
It tracks the size, health, governance and economic value of the world's forests
The 2026 edition focuses on Forest and Landscape Restoration (FLR)
It draws on the Global Forest Resources Assessment 2025 and country reports under the Kunming-
Montreal Global Biodiversity Framework
Key Highlights
Global Forest Status
Forest area: 4.14 billion hectares, covering 32% of global land.
Carbon stored: 714 gigatonnes.
Forests support up to 80% of terrestrial biodiversity.
Forest Loss and Degradation
1.56 billion hectares of land is degraded, including 460 million hectares of forest.
489 million hectares cleared since 1990.
Wildfires affect 127 million hectares annually.
Global Restoration Gap
Global pledges: Over 1 billion hectares.
Reported restoration: 44 million hectares under FRA 2025.
Gap to 2030 targets: 470 million hectares.
Restoration can generate up to $30 per $1 invested under favourable conditions.
India's Position
Country Area under restoration
India 9.8 million hectares
China 7.9 million hectares

Ethiopia 5.0 million hectares
India ranks first globally in active forest restoration under FRA 2025.
Under its 7th National Report to the CBD, India reported 18.9 million hectares under forest and
landscape restoration, ranking second after China.
Key initiatives include compensatory afforestation, agroforestry and Joint Forest Management.
Important Facts for Exams
FAO — founded 1945, headquartered at Rome
Kerala Launches India's First Ecosystem Red List Assessment
Source: The Hindu
Context of the News
The Kerala State Biodiversity Board (KSBB) has launched India's first State-level Red List Assessment of
Ecosystems (RLE). It will judge how close entire ecosystems are to collapse.
What Is It?
An assessment that checks the risk of collapse of whole ecosystems
Not individual species — this is the key difference
It uses the IUCN Red List of Ecosystems framework
Funded by the Kerala State Biodiversity Board
Duration: one year, covering all 14 districts of Kerala
How Is It Different From the Usual Red List?
The familiar IUCN Red List rates species — tiger, bustard, gharial
This one rates ecosystems — a forest type, a wetland, a grassland
A species can survive while the system it belongs to falls apart. Rating the ecosystem catches that
How Does It Classify Risk?
Using the five standard IUCN RLE criteria
These look at historical habitat loss, environmental degradation and risk of collapse
India Joins the Southern Indian Ocean Fisheries Agreement
Source: PIB
Context of the News
The Central Government approved India's accession to the Southern Indian Ocean Fisheries Agreement
(SIOFA). India moves up from a Cooperating Non-Contracting Party to a full Contracting Party.
What Is SIOFA?
A regional fisheries agreement, and also a Regional Fisheries Management Organisation (RFMO)
It works on the conservation and sustainable use of non-tuna fish in the high seas of the Southern
Indian Ocean
Headquarters: Saint-Denis, Réunion Island (France)
Run by a Meeting of the Parties, with a Scientific Committee and a Compliance Committee
Important Facts for Exams
India approved accession to SIOFA in October 2026, becoming a Contracting Party
India held Cooperating Non-Contracting Party status since 2022

SIOFA concluded at Rome, July 2006 under FAO; in force June 2012
Headquarters: Saint-Denis, Réunion Island (France)
Manages non-tuna, non-migratory stocks in the high seas of the Southern Indian Ocean
Species include orange roughy, alfonsino, toothfish, molluscs and crustaceans
India is the world's second-largest fish producer and second-largest aquaculture producer;
production reached 198 lakh tonnes in 2024-25
WINDS Weather Network
Source: The Hindu
Context of the News
India's WINDS project — a network of automatic rain gauges and weather stations to support crop
insurance — is more than a year behind schedule. Over 96 per cent of planned sensors are still not
installed.
What Is WINDS?
Weather Information Network and Data System
A nationwide network of weather sensors to supply hyper-local weather data
Its main purpose is computing insurance estimates and payouts under the Pradhan Mantri Fasal
Bima Yojana
Run by the Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare
Executed through the Mahalanobis National Crop Forecast Centre (MNCFC)
Implemented by States through private partners
Key Terms
ARG: Automatic Rain Gauge — measures rainfall and transmits the reading automatically
AWS: Automatic Weather Station — measures several weather parameters at once
Hyper-local data: Weather readings at village or panchayat level, rather than district level
Siting requirements: Technical rules on where an instrument may be placed, so readings are
accurate
Retrospective amendment: A rule change applied to decisions already taken under the earlier rule

The Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship (MSDE) signed a Shareholders’ Agreement with
ArcelorMittal Nippon Steel India and the governments of Andhra Pradesh and Gujarat.
The project will upgrade 9 Government Industrial Training Institutes (ITIs) in Surat and Visakhapatnam
clusters, with an approved investment of ₹440 crore.
Five ITIs in Surat will receive ₹240 crore, while four ITIs in Visakhapatnam will receive ₹200 crore.
Special Purpose Vehicles (SPVs) will manage the clusters, with AM/NS India holding a 51% stake.

5. SEBI Finalises Closing Auction Session Rules
The Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) retained the volume-weighted average price
(VWAP) of the final 30 minutes of regular trading to determine settlement prices for expiring stock and
index derivatives.
For index derivatives, settlement uses the VWAP of constituent securities during the final 30 minutes of
cash-market trading. For expiring stock derivatives, it uses the underlying stock's VWAP across
exchanges.
Most changes will take effect from 7 December 2026, while rules for transferring certain unexecuted
orders into the Closing Auction Session will begin on 8 February 2027.

Pradhan Mantri Samajik Sahayata Yojana
Source: PIB
Context of the News
The Union Cabinet has approved continuing the National Social Assistance Programme (NSAP) for five
more years, from FY2026-27 to 2030-31, with an outlay of ₹53,428 crore. The scheme has been renamed
Pradhan Mantri Samajik Sahayata Yojana (PMSSY).
What Is the Scheme?
It gives monthly pensions and social security assistance to poor and vulnerable people
Beneficiaries are BPL elderly persons, widows, persons with disabilities and households that lose
their breadwinner
It is non-contributory — the beneficiary pays nothing
It is 100 per cent centrally funded
It follows from Article 41 of the Constitution, a Directive Principle that asks the State to help citizens in
old age, sickness and disability

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 110 of 212
“Mark the bold line. It is bold for a reason.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Run by the Department of Rural Development, Ministry of Rural Development
What Are the New Names?
Old name New name
IGNOAPS (old age pension) Pradhan Mantri Vridhavastha Pension Yojana
IGNWPS (widow pension) Pradhan Mantri Vidhawa Pension Yojana
IGNDPS (disability pension) Pradhan Mantri Divyangjan Pension Yojana
NFBS (family benefit) Pradhan Mantri Parivar Labh Yojana
What Changed for Persons With Disabilities?
Disability threshold lowered
Earlier a person needed 80 per cent severe disability to qualify
Now 40 per cent benchmark disability is enough
This matches the Rights of Persons with Disabilities Act, 2016
Minimum age removed
Earlier a person had to be 18 years old
Now a person with benchmark disability is eligible from birth

1. India's Merchandise Exports Rise 23.04% in September 2026
India's merchandise exports rose 23.04% year-on-year to $44.4 billion in September 2026, supported
by petroleum products, electronic goods and chemicals.
Imports stood at $74.61 billion, while the merchandise trade deficit narrowed year-on-year to $30.21
billion, compared with $33 billion in September 2025.
Electronic goods exports grew 70.44% to $5.3 billion, petroleum product exports rose 31.9% to $6.2
billion, and chemical exports increased 24.21% to $3 billion.
2. India’s Unemployment Rate Remains at 5%
India's unemployment rate remained at 5% in September 2026, unchanged from August, according to
the Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) bulletin of the National Statistics Office.
The labour force participation rate declined slightly from 55.6% to 55.5%.
Unemployment was higher in urban areas at 6.9% than in rural areas at 4.2%. Overall unemployment
among women was 5.2%, compared with 4.9% among men.

NABARD  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
Agriculture
India's Ramsar Sites Reach 103
Source: News on Air
Context of the News
India now has 103 Ramsar sites. Two were added — Sukhna Wetland in Chandigarh and Sauj Jheel in
Uttar Pradesh.
Sukhna Wetland, Chandigarh
What is it?
A man-made reservoir and freshwater wetland on the edge of Chandigarh
Ramsar Site number 2602
Sits at the foot of the Shivalik range, the outer Himalayas
It acts as both a water regulator and an urban wildlife refuge
Sauj Jheel, Uttar Pradesh
What is it?
A perennial shallow floodplain lake
Ramsar Site number 2603
An Important Bird and Biodiversity Area (IBA)
Located in Mainpuri district, near Kishni, in the Indo-Gangetic floodplain
Important Facts for Exams
Mainpuri is the only district in India with two Ramsar sites — the other is Saman Bird Sanctuary
(2019)
Uttar Pradesh has 14 Ramsar sites, the highest for any state; Tamil Nadu is close behind
India has the third-highest number of Ramsar sites in the world, after the UK and Mexico, and the
most in Asia
The Ramsar Convention was signed at Ramsar, Iran, in 1971, and came into force in 1975. It is the
oldest of the modern global environmental treaties
India joined in 1982; its first sites were Chilika Lake (Odisha) and Keoladeo National Park (Rajasthan)
World Wetlands Day: 2 February
1. Cheruvayal Raman, Traditional Rice Conservationist, Passes Away
Cheruvayal Raman, known as “Nellachan” and the “Living Paddy Gene Bank,” passed away in Kerala at
the age of 74.

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 115 of 212
“Phone away, notes open. That is the whole trick.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
He preserved more than 45 traditional rice varieties through traditional farming in Wayanad and
distributed indigenous seeds to farmers free of cost.
He received the Padma Shri in 2023 and the National Plant Genome Saviour Award in 2016 for his
contribution to agricultural biodiversity.
Schemes
1. National Social Assistance Programme (NSAP) to Continue for Five Years
The Union Cabinet approved the continuation of the National Social Assistance Programme (NSAP) for
five years, from FY 2026–27 to FY 2030–31, with a reported allocation of ₹53,428 crore.
According to the supplied report, the programme will be renamed Pradhan Mantri Samajik Sahayata
Yojana (PMSSY), with changes to the names of its pension and family-benefit sub-schemes.
The programme provides social assistance to eligible elderly people, widows, persons with disabilities
and families facing the death of a primary breadwinner.
2. Credit Risk Insurance Support for Shipbuilding
The Ministry of Ports, Shipping and Waterways launched two credit risk insurance products on 13
October 2026 under the Shipbuilding Development Scheme (SbDS).
The Shipbuilding Project Credit Risk Insurance Policy (SbPCP) covers specified credit and payment risks
in export shipbuilding projects, while the Business Credit Shield Policy – Shipbuilders (BCSPS) covers
domestic shipbuilding projects.
The initiative aims to protect shipyards against payment delays, reduce financial risks and promote
indigenous shipbuilding.

1. India Ranks Third in Contracted Clean Hydrogen Volumes
India ranks third globally in cumulative contracted clean hydrogen volumes, according to Union

Clean hydrogen can reduce emissions in industries that are difficult to electrify and support India's

PUFFINS Telescope Mission
Source: The Hindu
Context of the News
Bengaluru-born astronomer Dr Ramya Anche has been selected as Principal Investigator of NASA's
$20-million PUFFINS space telescope mission.
What Is PUFFINS?
Full form: Polarimetry in the Ultraviolet to Find Features in INterStellar dust
A small satellite in low-Earth orbit
It carries a 10-inch ultraviolet telescope
It measures the polarisation of UV starlight as it passes through interstellar dust
Who Runs It?
Funded by NASA's Astrophysics Pioneers programme
Led by the University of Arizona's Steward Observatory
With the McDonald Observatory, University of Texas
What Makes It Notable?
It revives ultraviolet spectropolarimetry after nearly three decades
It targets dust grains smaller than 50 nanometres, which are very hard to study with normal optical or

infrared telescopes
It will study how elongated dust grains line up with the Milky Way's magnetic field — the change in
starlight polarisation reveals the shape of galactic magnetic fields
It will observe at least 70 distant stars, across stellar nurseries, reflection nebulae and the
Magellanic Clouds
Ground support comes from the 82-inch Otto Struve Telescope at McDonald Observatory

10 Years of the Kigali Amendment
Source: Indian Express
Context of the News
The Kigali Amendment to the Montreal Protocol completed 10 years on 15 October 2026. It aims to cut
hydrofluorocarbons (HFCs) and avoid up to 0.5°C of warming by 2100.
What Is It?
A legally binding amendment to the 1987 Montreal Protocol
It requires countries to gradually phase down the production and use of HFCs

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 118 of 212
“Phone away, notes open. That is the whole trick.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
HFCs are powerful greenhouse gases, used mainly in air conditioners and refrigerators
Important Facts for Exams
Kigali Amendment adopted 15 October 2016 at Kigali, Rwanda; in force 1 January 2019
First proposed in 2009 by the Federated States of Micronesia and Mauritius
It amends the Montreal Protocol, 1987, which itself sits under the Vienna Convention, 1985
Target: cut HFCs by over 80-85 per cent in three decades; avoid 0.3-0.5°C of warming by 2100
The Montreal Protocol phased out about 99 per cent of ozone-depleting substances
It is the only UN treaty ratified by every country in the world — all 198 parties
India ratified the Kigali Amendment in 2021
India follows the India Cooling Action Plan (2019) — the first country to produce one
World Ozone Day: 16 September, marking the signing of the Montreal Protocol

Agriculture
FSSAI Proposes Pesticide Residue Limits for Tea
Food Safety and Standards Authority of India (FSSAI) proposed maximum residue limits (MRLs) for
acetamiprid and imidacloprid in tea.
The proposed limits are 20 mg/kg for acetamiprid and 50 mg/kg for imidacloprid.
The acetamiprid limit is stricter than the limits mentioned for Japan (30 parts per million) and the
United States (50 parts per million).
The proposal aims to improve food safety, pesticide monitoring and regulatory clarity for tea growers
and processors. Pasted text.txt

crops and ensure 100% procurement at the Minimum Support Price (MSP).

Female Labour Force Participation in the North-East and Union Territories
The Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) 2025 shows that female labour force participation has
increased since 2019–20, but the improvement differs across States and Union Territories.
Rural female labour force participation increased from 33% to 45.9%, while the urban rate rose from
23.3% to 27.7%.
Arunachal Pradesh recorded the highest average annual percentage-point change in both rural
(+8.28) and urban (+5.24) participation.
Lakshadweep recorded negative changes in both rural (−2.34) and urban (−1.30) participation,
highlighting regional differences in women's economic participation. Pasted text.txt
WTO Raises Global Merchandise Trade Growth Forecast for 2026
The World Trade Organization (WTO) raised its forecast for global merchandise trade growth in
calendar year 2026 to 3.9%, up from its earlier estimate of 1.9%.
Artificial intelligence-related investments increased by 67% year-on-year in the first half of 2026 and
accounted for 47% of total merchandise trade growth, according to the report.
Global merchandise trade growth is projected at 4.1% in 2027, while services trade growth is forecast at
3.3% in 2026.
The WTO projected global gross domestic product (GDP) growth of 2.6% in 2026 and 2.9% in 2027.

India Approves Accession to the Southern Indian Ocean Fisheries Agreement
The Government of India approved accession to the Southern Indian Ocean Fisheries Agreement
(SIOFA) through the Ministry of Fisheries, Animal Husbandry and Dairying.
India had participated as a Cooperating Non-Contracting Party since 2022; full membership will
provide greater participation in fisheries-related decision-making.
Indian fishing vessels will be able to undertake authorised fishing for species such as squid, toothfish
and alfonsino within the agreement's jurisdiction.
The move aims to strengthen international fisheries cooperation, scientific research, sustainable
fishing and livelihood opportunities for fishers.

Overseas Citizenship of India (OCI)
Source: Indian Express
Context of the News
The FRRO reportedly issued a 'Leave India' notice to actor and OCI cardholder Imran Khan, after he took
part in a protest in Mumbai. The case has brought attention to what an OCI card does and does not
allow.
What Is OCI?
A special immigration status, introduced in 2005 under the Citizenship Act, 1955
Available to foreign nationals of Indian origin
Not available to those with roots in Pakistan or Bangladesh
Is an OCI Holder an Indian Citizen?
No. This is the core point.
Article 9 of the Constitution bars dual citizenship
An OCI cardholder stays a foreign national, holding a foreign passport
Despite the word "citizenship" in the name, it is essentially a lifelong visa
What Does an OCI Card Give You?
A lifelong, multiple-entry, multi-purpose visa to visit India
Exemption from registering with the FRRO, no matter how long the stay
Parity with NRIs in financial, economic and educational matters
But not for buying agricultural or plantation property
What Can an OCI Holder Not Do?
No political rights — under Section 7B of the Citizenship Act, 1955:
No Article 19 protections
Activities needing prior permission — under the March 2021 OCI notification:
Important Facts for Exams
OCI introduced in 2005, under the Citizenship Act, 1955
An OCI cardholder is a foreign national, not an Indian citizen
Article 9 bars dual citizenship
Section 7B denies voting rights, election candidacy and constitutional offices
Article 19 rights apply only to citizens; Articles 14 and 21 apply to all persons, including foreigners
Cannot buy agricultural or plantation land
The March 2021 notification requires prior permission for journalism, research, missionary work,
mountaineering and protected area visits

The PIO card scheme was merged into OCI in 2015

India–Mauritius Tax Treaty Introduces Principal Purpose Test
The Central Government notified amendments to the India–Mauritius Double Taxation Avoidance
Agreement (DTAA) on 13 October 2026.
The amendments introduce the Principal Purpose Test (PPT) to prevent misuse of tax treaty benefits.
Under the test, treaty benefits may be denied when obtaining those benefits is one of the principal
purposes of a transaction, unless granting them is consistent with the treaty's objectives.
Investments made before 1 April 2017 are exempt from the PPT, according to the supplied report; the
change aims to strengthen tax compliance while protecting legitimate treaty benefits. Pasted text.txt
NTPC Signs Agreement with Africa50 for Power Projects
NTPC Limited, a Maharatna Central Public Sector Enterprise under the Ministry of Power, signed a
cooperation agreement with Africa50.
The partnership aims to identify and develop commercially viable power projects across African
countries.
NTPC will contribute technical expertise in project assessment, engineering, construction and
operations, while Africa50 will support project identification, government coordination and investment
structuring.
The agreement will also explore cross-border electricity trading to improve regional power
connectivity and electricity availability. Pasted text.txt
Social Issue
Supreme Court Recognises Internet Access as a Fundamental Right
The Supreme Court stated that the right to access and use the Internet is a fundamental right under
Article 21 read with Article 19(1)(a) of the Constitution.
The ruling referred to the 2020 Anuradha Bhasin v. Union of India judgment, which recognised
constitutional protection for freedom of speech and expression through the Internet.
The Court emphasised that children should not be treated as the problem merely because they may
encounter objectionable online material.
The report highlights the responsibility of individuals who create or share objectionable content and
intermediaries that fail to remove it. Pasted text.txt

Bengaluru Scientists Study Solar Eruptions Using Aditya-L1 and Proba-3
Scientists from the Indian Institute of Astrophysics and international collaborators studied coronal
mass ejections (CMEs) using data from India's Aditya-L1 and Europe's Proba-3 missions.
They combined observations from the Visible Emission Line Coronagraph (VELC) aboard Aditya-L1 and
the ASPIICS instrument aboard Proba-3.
The joint observations examined the mass, density and energy of solar eruptions in the region close to
the Sun.
The study demonstrates the value of combining observations from different space missions to
understand solar activity.

Agriculture
Agrivoltaics in India
Source: Indian Express
Context of the News
India hosts the 7th AgriVoltaics World Conference at Bharat Mandapam, New Delhi, from 21 to 23
October. The theme is "Powering the Global South". The two Union Ministers write about how agrivoltaics
can give farmers a second income from the same land.
What Is Agrivoltaics?
Growing crops and generating solar power on the same land
Using structures and panel spacing designed to allow farming underneath
Also called AgriPV
What Is the Microclimate Benefit?
The panels create shade, which protects crops against:
Heat stress
Drought
Excessive solar radiation
Hail
Important Facts for Exams
7th AgriVoltaics World Conference, Bharat Mandapam, New Delhi, 21-23 October 2026; theme
"Powering the Global South"
Design standard: panels should let 70 per cent or more of natural radiation reach the crop canopy
Crop criteria: shade tolerance, morphological plasticity, stable productivity under intermittent
radiation
Centre of Excellence on Agri-Photovoltaics at ICAR-IARI, New Delhi, set up 2025, 100 kW; run by ICAR
and NISE under MNRE
PM-KUSUM solarises irrigation; PM-KUSUM 2.0 will include a dedicated agrivoltaics component
PM-KUSUM has three components: Component A (decentralised solar plants on barren land),
Component B (standalone solar pumps), Component C (solarising existing grid-connected pumps)
ISA — International Solar Alliance, launched by India and France at COP21, Paris, 2015; headquartered
at Gurugram
Authors: Pralhad Joshi, Union Minister of New and Renewable Energy, and Shivraj Singh Chouhan,
Union Minister of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare

Kufri Jamunia
Source: PIB
Context of the News
ICAR-Central Potato Research Institute has highlighted Kufri Jamunia, a biofortified purple potato
developed in India, as a step against hidden hunger.
What Is It?
A nutrient-dense potato variety
Dark purple skin and purple flesh
Bred to deliver higher levels of antioxidants, vitamins and micronutrients through everyday food
Who Developed It and When?
ICAR-Central Potato Research Institute (CPRI), Shimla
Released in 2024
What Came Before It?
Variety Year Feature
Kufri Neelkanth 2018 Purple skin, high antioxidants
Kufri Manik 2021 Red skin, yellow flesh
Kufri Jamunia 2024 Deep purple skin and flesh, high anthocyanin
Important Facts for Exams
Kufri Jamunia — biofortified purple potato from ICAR-CPRI, Shimla; released 2024
Biofortification: Breeding a crop to contain more nutrients, rather than adding nutrients afterwards
Anthocyanins 42.05 mg, Vitamin C 42.75 mg, iron 3.22 mg, zinc 2.28 mg per 100 g
ICAR-CPRI is at Shimla, Himachal Pradesh; all its varieties carry the Kufri prefix
India is the world's second-largest potato producer after China; Uttar Pradesh leads, followed by
West Bengal and Bihar
Other biofortified releases in India: iron-rich pearl millet, zinc-rich wheat and rice, protein-rich maize
and Kufri Lima
Biofortification is promoted under the Poshan Abhiyaan and Krishonnati Yojana

Created under the Biological Diversity Act, 2002

Context of the News
A study in PLOS One found that Asiatic lions have permanently reclaimed Barda Wildlife Sanctuary in
Gujarat, after nearly 144 years.
What Is Barda?
A hilly forest sanctuary of about 192.31 sq km
In the Saurashtra region of Gujarat
Across Porbandar and Devbhumi Dwarka districts
Barely 15 km from the coastal town of Porbandar
Designated as the second home for the Asiatic lion
Important Facts for Exams
Barda Wildlife Sanctuary — 192.31 sq km, in Porbandar and Devbhumi Dwarka, Gujarat; notified
February 1979
Lions have returned after nearly 144 years; study published in PLOS One
Asiatic lion (Panthera leo persica) — IUCN Endangered, CITES Appendix I, Schedule I
The Asiatic lion is found only in Gujarat — the last wild population in the world
Gir National Park and Wildlife Sanctuary is the primary habitat; the 2025 census put the population
at 891
Project Lion was launched in 2020

Reports & Articles
India's Reservoir Storage Status 2026
Released by: Central Water Commission (CWC), based on its Reservoir Storage Bulletin dated 15
October 2026.
National storage: India's 178 major reservoirs held 126.717 billion cubic metres (BCM), equal to 68.58% of
their total usable capacity.
Year-on-year decline: Storage was 24.53% lower than the corresponding period a year earlier and
16.44% below normal.
Regional differences: The Pennar basin recorded a 72.32% deficit and the Cauvery basin a 60.50%
deficit, while the Swarnarekha, Narmada and Mahanadi basins had above-normal storage.

Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS), September 2026
Released by: National Statistical Office (NSO), Ministry of Statistics and Programme Implementation
(MoSPI), through its 18th monthly bulletin.
Labour Force Participation Rate (LFPR): India's overall LFPR rose to 55.5%, compared with 55.3% in

Banking and Finance
SEBI Revises Closing Auction Session Framework
Regulator: Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) revised the Closing Auction Session (CAS)
framework for the equity cash market in October 2026.
Effective date: The revised framework is scheduled to take effect from 7 December 2026.
New timing: The Closing Auction Session will run from 3:30 p.m. to 3:40 p.m., with three phases for
reference-price calculation, order entry and final limit-order entry.
Order rules: During the final phase, only limit orders will be allowed; market orders cannot be modified
or cancelled. The order-entry session will close randomly between 3:38 p.m. and 3:40 p.m.
Finance Receives In-Principle Approval for MF Lite AMC
Company: 1 Finance Private Limited received in-principle approval from SEBI to establish an Asset
Management Company (AMC) under the Mutual Funds Lite (MF Lite) framework.
Investment products: The proposed AMC will focus on passive investment products, mainly index funds
and exchange-traded funds (ETFs).
Regulatory framework: The MF Lite framework was introduced by SEBI on 31 December 2024 to facilitate
passive mutual fund businesses.
Financial requirements: The supplied report states a minimum net worth of ₹35 crore, potentially
reducible to ₹25 crore after five consecutive profitable years, subject to applicable regulatory
conditions.

Basawan: Mughal Painter
Source: Times of India
Context of the News
A nearly 450-year-old Mughal painting attributed to Basawan, titled "A Family of Cheetahs in a Rocky
Landscape", sold at Christie's in London for £10.245 million. It came from the collection of Prince
Sadruddin Aga Khan.
Who Was Basawan?
A leading Indian painter of the 16th century
A master in the imperial Mughal atelier under Emperor Akbar
Most active between 1575 and 1580
Important Facts for Exams
"A Family of Cheetahs in a Rocky Landscape", attributed to Basawan, sold at Christie's London for
£10.245 million
From the collection of Prince Sadruddin Aga Khan, assembled between the 1960s and 1980s
Basawan worked in Akbar's atelier, active around 1575-1580
His contemporary was Daswanth; both are praised in the Ain-i-Akbari by Abul Fazl
Mughal painting began under Humayun, who brought the Persian artists Mir Sayyid Ali and Abd-us-
Samad from Persia

Major works of Akbar's atelier: Hamzanama, Akbarnama, Razmnama (a Persian translation of the
Mahabharata) and Tutinama
Jahangir's reign is considered the peak of Mughal painting, especially natural history studies; Ustad

Aditya-L1
Source: The Hindu
Context of the News
Scientists have produced Doppler velocity images of the solar corona, using data from the Visible
Emission Line Coronagraph (VELC) on India's solar mission Aditya-L1. These images can show which
direction a solar eruption is travelling — something not available until now.
Important Facts for Exams
Aditya-L1 — India's first solar mission, launched 2 September 2023 by PSLV-C57
It was placed in a halo orbit around Lagrange Point 1 (L1) in January 2024
L1 lies about 1.5 million km from Earth, towards the Sun. A satellite there can watch the Sun
continuously, without eclipses
VELC — Visible Emission Line Coronagraph; the primary payload, developed by the Indian Institute of
Astrophysics, Bengaluru
Aditya-L1 carries seven payloads — four observe the Sun directly, three study particles and fields at L1
Principal Investigator, VELC: R. Ramesh, IIA
Other solar missions: NASA's Parker Solar Probe, the ESA-NASA Solar Orbiter and SOHO
The World Bank's October 2026: Poverty and Equity Brief
Source: Business Standard
Context of the News
The World Bank's October 2026 Poverty and Equity Brief shows poverty has fallen sharply in India. But
the editorial argues the next stage depends on creating better jobs, not just more jobs.
What Does the Poverty Data Show?
Measure 2011 2023
Below $4.20 a day (lower-middle-income line) 57.7 per cent 16.1 per cent
Below $3 a day (extreme poverty) — 2.6 per cent
496 million people moved above the $4.20 line
Projected to fall further — 14.2 per cent (2026) to 12.1 per cent (2028)
Key Terms
PPP: Purchasing Power Parity — adjusts incomes for what money actually buys in each country
Real earnings: Wages adjusted for inflation
Low-productivity work: Jobs that create little value, so wages stay low even when employment rises
Labour-intensive manufacturing: Industries that need many workers relative to machinery
FLFPR: Female Labour Force Participation Rate
Median earnings: The middle value — half earn more, half earn less
Important Facts for Exams

Global Hunger Index 2026
Source: Global Hunger Index
Context of the News
The Global Hunger Index 2026 was released on 14 October 2026, ahead of World Food Day (16 October),
at a hybrid event in Berlin. India ranked 100th out of 122 countries, with a score of 24.1 — in the "serious"
hunger category.
Who Publishes It?
Welthungerhilfe (WHH), a German humanitarian organisation
With the Institute for International Law of Peace and Armed Conflict (IFHV)
And Helvetas

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 137 of 212
“One clear hour a day outruns a distracted five.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Title of the 2026 report: "No Security Without Food Security"
How Is the Score Calculated?
Four indicators, with different weights:
Indicator Weight What it measures
Undernourishment One-third Share of the population whose calorie intake is insufficient
Child stunting One-sixth Children under 5 with low height for their age
Child wasting One-sixth Children under 5 with low weight for their height
Child mortality One-third Children who die before their fifth birthday
How Is the Score Read?
The scale runs from 0 to 100
0 is the best score — no hunger
100 is the worst
What Is the Global Trend?
The global score rose from 17.6 in 2018 to 18.1 in 2026
That is a moderate level of hunger globally — but it has worsened
Hunger levels got worse in 32 countries
57 countries are not on track to reach low hunger by 2030
Which Countries Did Best?
24 countries scored below 5.0, including Armenia, Belarus, Bulgaria, Chile, Costa Rica and Croatia
Moldova 25th (5.0)
Mongolia 26th (5.3)
Argentina, Brazil and Azerbaijan shared 27th (5.5)
Which Countries Did Worst?
Rank Country Score
122 Somalia 47.0
121 DR Congo 39.5
120 Chad 38.0
119 Madagascar 36.5
118 Haiti 34.4
Important Facts for Exams
GHI 2026 — India 100th of 122, score 24.1, in the "serious" category
Released 14 October 2026 in Berlin, ahead of World Food Day, 16 October
Published by Welthungerhilfe, with IFHV and Helvetas; title "No Security Without Food Security"
Four indicators: undernourishment (1/3), child stunting (1/6), child wasting (1/6), child mortality (1/3)
Global score: 17.6 (2018) → 18.1 (2026); worse in 32 countries; 57 countries off track for 2030
Worst: Somalia 47.0, DR Congo 39.5, Chad 38.0, Madagascar 36.5, Haiti 34.4
GHI severity bands: low (under 9.9), moderate (10-19.9), serious (20-34.9), alarming (35-49.9),
extremely alarming (50 and above)
India has consistently rejected the GHI methodology. The government's objections are that three of
four indicators cover only children under five, the undernourishment figure rests on a small opinion

poll sample, and the index does not capture India's food security programmes
India's own nutrition data comes from the NFHS; NFHS-5 recorded stunting 35.5 per cent, wasting 19.3

Economy
India and Mauritius Amend Double Taxation Avoidance Agreement
Notification: The Central Government notified amendments to the India–Mauritius Double Taxation
Avoidance Agreement (DTAA) on 13 October 2026.
Principal Purpose Test (PPT): The test helps prevent misuse of tax treaty benefits when obtaining those
benefits is one of the principal purposes of a transaction.
Objective: The amendment aims to reduce tax avoidance and strengthen compliance while
preserving legitimate treaty benefits.
Exemption: According to the supplied report, investments made before 1 April 2017 are exempt from the
PPT.
NTPC Partners with Africa50 for African Power Projects
Agreement: NTPC Limited signed a cooperation agreement with Africa50 to identify and develop
commercially viable power projects across African countries.
NTPC's role: It will contribute technical expertise in project assessment, engineering, construction and
operations.
Africa50's role: It will support project identification, government coordination and investment
structuring.
Objective: The partnership also explores cross-border electricity trading to improve regional power
connectivity and electricity availability.
India Supports Indigenous Drone and Quantum-Secure Technologies
Indigenous innovation: The Technology Development Board's agreements with ideaForge and QNu
Labs support domestic technology development in aerial logistics and quantum security.
Drone logistics: The YETI vehicle is designed for remote and high-altitude deliveries, with a reported
payload capacity of 200 kilograms.
Quantum security: The QNu Labs project targets secure identity, location integrity, communications
networks and time synchronisation.
Economic significance: Such technologies can support domestic manufacturing, strategic
infrastructure and reduced dependence on foreign technology.
Social Issue
Internet Access as a Fundamental Right for Children
Supreme Court ruling: The Supreme Court of India affirmed that Internet access is protected as a
fundamental right, including for children, irrespective of age.
Constitutional provisions: The ruling relates to Article 19(1)(a), which protects freedom of speech and

expression, and Article 21, which protects life and personal liberty.
Child safety: The Court emphasised that children should not be excluded from Internet access simply
because harmful content exists online.
Key principle: Efforts to improve digital safety should address the sources of harmful material and
those responsible for it, rather than treating children themselves as the problem.

Miscellaneous General Awareness
World Students' Day 2026
Date: World Students' Day is observed on 15 October, marking the birth anniversary of Dr. A. P. J. Abdul
Kalam.
2026 milestone: The year marked his 95th birth anniversary.
Scientific contributions: Dr. Kalam contributed to India's space and defence programmes through the
Indian Space Research Organisation (ISRO) and Defence Research and Development Organisation
(DRDO).
Key initiatives: His contributions included the Satellite Launch Vehicle-III, Agni and Prithvi missile
programmes, and Technology Vision 2020.
International Day of the Intangible Cultural Heritage 2026
Date: Observed on 17 October; 2026 marked its third observance.
UNESCO decision: The day was proclaimed in November 2023 through Resolution 42 C/34, with the first
observance held in 2024.
Purpose: It promotes awareness about protecting living cultural traditions, including music, dance,
rituals, crafts and oral traditions.
Convention: It commemorates the 2003 Convention for the Safeguarding of the Intangible Cultural
Heritage, which entered into force in 2006.

Agriculture
Upgraded Agri Welfare Portal Launched
Source: News on Air
Context of the News
Union Agriculture Minister Shivraj Singh Chouhan launched the upgraded Agri Welfare Portal. It gives
farmers single-window access to government schemes, financial help and farming information.
What Does Single-Window Access Mean Here?
The portal pulls together three major systems:
PM-KISAN — the income support scheme
PMFBY — crop insurance
AgriStack — the farmer and land database
A farmer can check scheme details, eligibility and apply in one place, instead of visiting separate
portals.
What Services Does It Provide?
Weather conditions
Scientific farming practices
Seed and fertiliser availability
Agricultural market prices
Financial assistance paid through Direct Benefit Transfer
Important Facts for Exams
Agri Welfare Portal upgraded and launched by Shivraj Singh Chouhan, Ministry of Agriculture and
Farmers' Welfare
Integrates PM-KISAN, PMFBY and AgriStack
Built per DBIM 3.0 and GIGW 3.0, both from MeitY
Connects FPOs, PACS, scientists, researchers and startups
Training through Krishi Vigyan Kendras
PM-KISAN pays ₹6,000 a year in three instalments of ₹2,000

RBI Monetary Policy Committee (MPC) Minutes
Repo rate: The October 2026 Monetary Policy Committee (MPC) meeting minutes reported a
unanimous decision to raise the repo rate by 25 basis points to 5.5%.

round price rises, in which an initial price increase leads to further increases in wages and other prices.

RBI  AND  NABARD  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
Three New Indian Sites on the UNESCO Tentative List
Source: Times of India
Context of the News
India's Permanent Delegation to UNESCO announced that three sites have been added to the World
Heritage Tentative List — the Karst-mud-volcano Complex of Baratang, the Andaman Subduction
Volcanoes, and the Sirpur Cultural and Archaeological Landscape.
What Is a Tentative List?
A list of sites a country intends to nominate for the World Heritage List in future years
Being on the tentative list is compulsory before any site can be formally evaluated and nominated
Site 1: Karst-Mud-Volcano Complex of Baratang
A natural site on Baratang Island, Andaman and Nicobar Islands
Combines limestone karst caves and active mud volcanoes
Site 2: Andaman Subduction Volcanoes
A natural serial site made of Narcondam Island and Barren Island
The only Quaternary-period subduction volcanoes in the inner volcanic arc of the 5,000 km Burma-
Andaman-Java subduction complex

Site 3: Sirpur Cultural and Archaeological Landscape
A cultural site on the eastern bank of the Mahanadi River
In Mahasamund district, Chhattisgarh
It was Shripur, the ancient capital of the Kosala Kingdom, under the Sharabhapuriya and
Panduvamshi dynasties
UNEP Emissions Gap Report 2026
Source: Times of India
Context of the News
UNEP's annual Emissions Gap Report found that India's greenhouse gas emissions fell in 2025 over the
previous year. UNEP credits the country's structural shift towards renewables, and in part weather
effects.
Key Highlights
What Is Notable About India's Numbers?
India recorded a decrease in emissions in 2025
This marks a break in the trend after a long period of rapid growth
India is the third-largest emitter in total greenhouse gas emissions
But its per capita emission is:
Less than half the global average
Substantially lower than China and the United States
Important Facts for Exams
UNEP Emissions Gap Report, released October 2026
India's emissions fell in 2025 — a break after years of rapid growth
India is the third-largest total emitter; per capita emissions are less than half the global average
EU and Russia also saw declines; China and the US saw increases
Global emissions in 2025 were similar to 2024
1.5°C without overshoot is no longer achievable; current policies point to about 2.8°C this century
Baseline for warming: 1850-1900
UNEP — United Nations Environment Programme, founded 1972 after the Stockholm Conference;
headquartered at Nairobi, Kenya
The Emissions Gap Report is published annually, ahead of the COP climate meeting
Paris Agreement, 2015: hold warming well below 2°C, pursuing efforts to limit it to 1.5°C
The top emitters in order: China, United States, India, EU, Russia
Pumped Storage Hydropower in India
Source: PIB
Context of the News
The Ministry of Power and the Central Electricity Authority highlighted the growing role of Pumped
Storage Projects (PSPs), as India crossed 300 GW of non-fossil capacity on the way to its 500 GW target
by 2030.
What Is Pumped Storage?
Think of it as a large water battery.
Two reservoirs are built at different heights

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 144 of 212
“Discipline is choosing tomorrow over tonight.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
When power is cheap or surplus — such as during peak solar hours at midday — water is pumped up
to the higher reservoir
When demand peaks in the evening, or renewables fall short, the water is released downhill through
reversible pump-turbines to generate power
The energy is stored as gravitational potential energy
What Are the Three Types?
Type Upper reservoir Lower reservoir
On-stream On a natural river or stream On the same or connected river
Off-stream open-loop Away from natural flows On an existing river or reservoir, or the reverse
Off-stream closed-loop Completely away from natural flows Completely away from natural flows
Where Does India Stand Globally?
Rank Country
1 China
2 Japan
3 United States
4 India
Economy
World Bank Group’s First Digital Strategy
Launch: The World Bank Group launched its first comprehensive digital strategy at the Global Artificial
Intelligence and Digital Summit 2026 in Seoul, South Korea.
Target: The strategy aims to benefit 1 billion people by 2030 through better internet connectivity,
affordable digital access, digital services and skills, and improved artificial intelligence readiness.
Digital divide: In 2025, more than 2 billion people worldwide remained offline. Internet usage was 23% in
low-income countries, compared with 94% in high-income countries.
New initiative: The AI Infrastructure and Readiness Initiative will initially focus on Africa and Latin
America to improve access to affordable computing infrastructure.

International Day for the Eradication of Poverty 2026
Date: Observed every year on 17 October by the United Nations (UN).
2026–2028 theme: “United for Peace and Human Rights.”
Origin: On 17 October 1987, more than 100,000 people gathered at Trocadéro Plaza in Paris to honour
victims of poverty, hunger and violence.
UN recognition: The United Nations General Assembly adopted Resolution A/RES/47/196 on 22
December 1992. The first UN-recognised observance took place in 1993.

Date: Observed annually on 21 October to honour police personnel who died in the line of duty.

and flagged off a nationwide motorcycle rally themed “Fight Against Drugs.”

Gandhi.
First observance: National Solidarity Day was first observed on 20 October 1966.

DRDO Develops Portable ELINT System
Development: The Defence Research and Development Organisation (DRDO), through its Defence
Electronics Research Laboratory in Hyderabad, initiated the development of man-portable Electronic
Intelligence (ELINT) modules.
Purpose: The system is intended to detect, intercept and analyse signals emitted by enemy radars and
other electronic systems.
Technology: Antennas, receivers, digital signal processing and machine learning will help separate
signals and identify radar signatures.
Other development: DRDO also developed indigenous armour panels around 20% lighter than existing
systems, designed to withstand 30 mm armour-piercing ammunition and meet the specified STANAG
4569 Level 6 protection standard.

Scientific publication: The findings were published in the International Journal of Tropical Insect
Science. ZSI, established in 1916 and headquartered in Kolkata, studies and documents India’s animal
diversity.

Miscellaneous General Awareness
Sirpur Archaeological Site Moves Closer to UNESCO World Heritage Status
Location: Sirpur is situated on the eastern bank of the Mahanadi River in Mahasamund district,
Chhattisgarh.
UNESCO status: The site has been added to UNESCO's Tentative List, an important step towards
possible World Heritage Site recognition.
Historical importance: Its archaeological remains date from the 5th to the 12th century CE and
represent Hindu, Buddhist and Jain traditions within a unified urban landscape.
Next step: Authorities must prepare and submit a detailed nomination dossier to UNESCO for
consideration.
Japan's Martian Moons eXploration (MMX) Mission
Space agency: Japan Aerospace Exploration Agency (JAXA) is leading the robotic sample-return
mission, with participation from NASA, the European Space Agency, France's CNES and Germany's DLR.
Mission targets: The spacecraft will study Phobos and conduct flybys of Deimos, the two moons of
Mars.
Sample return: It aims to collect at least 10 grams of surface material from Phobos and return it to
Earth in 2031.
Scientific objective: The mission seeks to understand how Mars's moons formed and gain insights into
the early evolution of the Solar System.
Ustad Bismillah Khan Yuva Puraskar 2024 and 2025
Award ceremony: The Union Minister for Culture and Tourism conferred the awards for 2024 and 2025
on 106 young performing artists in New Delhi.
Established: The award was instituted in 2006 in memory of Ustad Bismillah Khan, the renowned
shehnai maestro and Bharat Ratna recipient.
Administered by: The Sangeet Natak Akademi, an autonomous organisation under the Ministry of
Culture.
Purpose: It recognises promising young performers and encourages the continuation of India's music,
dance, theatre and other performing arts traditions.
India's Ramsar Site Count Reaches 103
New additions: Sukhna Wetland in Chandigarh and Sauj Jheel in Uttar Pradesh were reported as newly
added Ramsar sites.
Sukhna Wetland: It supports wetland habitats and resident and migratory birds along the Central
Asian Flyway.
Sauj Jheel: Located in the Ganga floodplain, it is an Important Bird Area that supports vulnerable bird
species.
State ranking: According to the supplied report, Uttar Pradesh has 14 Ramsar sites, the second-highest
number among Indian States after Tamil Nadu.

Solar Eruptions Studied Using Aditya-L1 and Proba-3
Research: Scientists from Bengaluru and international collaborators studied coronal mass ejections
(CMEs), large eruptions of plasma and magnetic fields from the Sun.
Missions: The research combined observations from India's Aditya-L1 mission and Europe's Proba-3
mission.
Instruments: The study used the Visible Emission Line Coronagraph (VELC) aboard Aditya-L1 and the
ASPIICS instrument aboard Proba-3.
Significance: Combining observations from the two missions helps scientists study solar eruptions and
understand their mass, density and energy.

SEBI Eases Early Pay-In Rules for Share Delivery
The Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) has eased rules for Early Pay-In (EPI), which allows
investors to deliver shares before the normal settlement date.
Shares delivered early can be used to meet the margin requirement for the related sale.
The revised framework is scheduled to take effect on 22 February 2027.
Brokers and clearing corporations have four months to update their systems.

CBDT Introduces Form 173A for Tax Exemption Claims

Income-tax (Seventh Amendment) Rules, 2026.
It aims to standardise reporting and improve verification of specified tax exemption claims on interest
and capital gains from government securities.
The form applies to eligible Foreign Institutional Investors (FIIs) and the Bank for International
Settlements (BIS), subject to prescribed conditions.
Part A records investor details, while Part B covers securities, holdings, transactions, interest income
and capital gains.

The 17th edition of UNEP's annual assessment

1.5°C without overshoot is no longer possible
Global emissions: about 58.4 GtCO₂e (2025), broadly flat

What Is eMudhra?
Founded in 2008
A CCA-licensed digital identity and security firm under the IT Act, 2000
Holds nearly 38 per cent of India's digital signature certificate market
Issues TLS/SSL certificates and code-signing certificates
What does eMudhra do?
Digital Signature Certificates (DSC): Used for online activities such as Goods and Services Tax (GST)
filings, company filings and e-tendering.
Electronic signatures: Help organisations approve and sign documents without printing them.
Digital identity and verification: Help businesses verify customers and manage secure access.
What is a Digital Signature Certificate?
A Digital Signature Certificate (DSC) is an electronic certificate that helps verify the identity of a person
or organisation and supports secure digital signing. For example, a company may use a DSC to sign
documents while filing returns on a government portal instead of signing paper documents physically
Economy
India and New Zealand Sign MoC on Electronic Origin Data Exchange
India and New Zealand signed a Memorandum of Cooperation (MoC) in October 2026 to exchange
electronic data on the origin of goods traded between them.
The arrangement supports customs coordination under the India–New Zealand Free Trade Agreement
(FTA), which, according to the supplied news, came into force on 20 October 2026.
Rules of Origin determine whether goods qualify for preferential or reduced customs duties under an
FTA.
The agreement aims to reduce customs clearance time, transaction costs and information gaps,
helping trade and Indian exports such as textiles and engineering goods.
UN Biodiversity Summit to Review 2030 Goals
Conference: Representatives from 196 countries, Indigenous peoples, local communities and other
stakeholders gathered in Yerevan, Armenia, for the 17th United Nations biodiversity conference.
Main objective: To review global progress towards biodiversity conservation targets for 2030 and
assess the action needed to address the decline in biodiversity caused by human activities.
Key concern: Habitat destruction, pollution, climate change and the overuse of natural resources
threaten biodiversity and ecosystem stability.
Significance: The conference provides an opportunity to strengthen international cooperation and
accelerate implementation of the Kunming–Montreal Global Biodiversity Framework.

Graded Response Action Plan (GRAP) Stage I Invoked in Delhi-NCR
Trigger: The Commission for Air Quality Management (CAQM) invoked Stage I of the Graded Response
Action Plan (GRAP) after the Air Quality Index (AQI) entered the Poor category (201–300).
Purpose: GRAP is an emergency framework that introduces progressively stricter measures to control
air pollution across Delhi and the National Capital Region (NCR).
Background: GRAP was formally notified on 12 January 2017 by the Ministry of Environment, Forest and
Climate Change, following Supreme Court directions in M. C. Mehta v. Union of India.
Four stages: Stage I applies to Poor air quality (201–300), Stage II to Very Poor (301–400), Stage III to
Severe (401–450), and Stage IV to Severe+ (above 450).
BHASHINI and IISc Develop Text-to-Speech Models for Sourashtra
The Digital India BHASHINI Division and the Indian Institute of Science (IISc), Bengaluru, developed text-
to-speech models for Sourashtra, a minority Indo-Aryan language spoken mainly in Tamil Nadu.
The four models use the Variational Inference with adversarial learning for end-to-end Text-to-Speech
(VITS) architecture and generate speech at 22,050 hertz.
The training corpus contains over 28 hours of recordings, with approximately 10,000 male and 11,400
female utterances.
The models are available through BHASHINI's AIKosh platform and can support language learning,
accessibility, digital content creation and language preservation.

Vummidi Ethiraj, Craftsman Behind the Sengol, Passes Away
Renowned jeweller and craftsman Vummidi Ethiraj passed away at the age of 99 in Chennai in
October 2026.
He helped craft the historic Sengol, a silver sceptre coated with gold, in 1947 for presentation to India's
first Prime Minister, Jawaharlal Nehru.
The Sengol symbolised the transfer of power from British rule to independent India and features a
Nandi (bull) figure at the top.
On 28 May 2023, Prime Minister Narendra Modi ceremonially installed the Sengol in India's new
Parliament building.
Former Prasar Bharati Chairman Releases Book on Indian TV News
Former Prasar Bharati Chairman Surya Prakash released Harsaran Bir Kaur Pandey's book, The
Evolution of TV News in India: From Doordarshan to Digital Disruption – An Insider’s Account.
Published by Om Books International, the book traces nearly five decades of Indian television
journalism.
It covers the growth of Doordarshan, satellite television, private news channels, 24-hour news and
digital media.
The book also discusses television coverage of major events, including the Emergency of 1975–1977,
the Jayaprakash Narayan Movement and Operation Blue Star.
World Iodine Deficiency Day 2026 – 21 October
World Iodine Deficiency Day, also known as Global Iodine Deficiency Disorders Prevention Day, is
observed annually on 21 October.
It raises awareness about iodine deficiency and its effects on health, growth and brain development.
Iodine is essential for producing the thyroid hormones triiodothyronine (T3) and thyroxine (T4), which
regulate metabolism and development.
Global efforts to prevent iodine deficiency gained momentum at the 1990 World Summit for Children,
with support from the World Health Organization (WHO) and UNICEF.
