Khajuraho's Medieval Temples
Source: Indian Express
Context of the News
The ASI plans to restore the headless sandstone Vishnu idol at Khajuraho's Javari Temple, by re-
attaching the head recently found in an ASI repository.
What Are the Khajuraho Temples?
A group of medieval Hindu and Jain temples
They represent the peak of Nagara-style temple architecture
Known for their soaring shikharas, mathematical proportions and intricate sculptural reliefs
Chhatarpur district, Madhya Pradesh
Against the backdrop of the Vindhya range
Who Built Them and When?
The Chandela dynasty
Built mainly between 950 and 1050 CE
They reflected the rulers' authority, religious devotion and artistic ambition
Important Facts for Exams
Khajuraho — Chhatarpur district, Madhya Pradesh, near the Vindhya range
Built 950-1050 CE by the Chandela dynasty
About 85 temples originally over 13 sq km; 20-23 survive, in three groups
Rediscovered by Captain T. S. Burt (1838); documented by Alexander Cunningham and F. C. Maisey
UNESCO World Heritage Site, 1986
The Kandariya Mahadeva Temple is the largest and most ornate, attributed to Vidyadhara
Temples are built of sandstone, mostly without mortar, using interlocking stone
The Eastern Group includes Jain temples such as Parshvanatha and Adinatha, alongside Hindu ones
The Chandelas ruled Bundelkhand from the 9th to 13th centuries; their fort capital was Kalinjar
Shamonda Virus
Source: TH
Context of the News
The WHO confirmed the first ever documented human infection and death from the Shamonda virus.
The patient was immunocompromised, in Lyon, France.
What Is the Virus?
A vector-borne animal virus
Belongs to the Simbu serogroup, genus Orthobunyavirus, family Peribunyaviridae
Closely related to the Schmallenberg virus

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 162 of 212
“Sleep is study too. A rested mind remembers.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
It mainly infects ruminants
Important Facts for Exams
Shamonda virus — first human infection and death confirmed by the WHO, in Lyon, France; patient
was immunocompromised
Simbu serogroup, genus Orthobunyavirus, family Peribunyaviridae; related to Schmallenberg virus
First isolated in cattle in Nigeria, 1965
Spread by Culicoides midges
Causes fever, falling milk yield, foetal deformities and abortions in ruminants
Has spread to over a dozen European countries, including France, Germany, Spain, Ireland
Not listed by WOAH or under EU Animal Health Law, so reporting is not mandatory
WOAH — World Organisation for Animal Health, founded 1924, headquartered at Paris
The One Health approach links human, animal and environmental health. India has a National One
Health Mission
Other Culicoides-borne diseases: bluetongue and African horse sickness

Source: European Parliament
Context of the News
The European Parliament announced the International Criminal Court (ICC) and its officials as the
collective winners of the 2026 Sakharov Prize for Freedom of Thought, on 22 October 2026.
What Is the Sakharov Prize?
The European Union's highest honour for human rights
Awarded annually to individuals, groups or organisations
For exceptional contribution to human rights, freedom of thought, democracy and the international
rule of law
Who Won in 2026?
The International Criminal Court and its personnel — judges, prosecutors and investigators
Under ICC President Tomoko Akane

2026 Sakharov Prize awarded collectively to the International Criminal Court and its officials

First awarded jointly to Nelson Mandela and Anatoly Marchenko

Andrei Sakharov won the Nobel Peace Prize in 1975
The ICC was created by the Rome Statute, adopted 1998, in force 2002; headquartered at The Hague
India is not a party to the Rome Statute. Nor are the United States, China or Russia

SDAL Delivers Nagastra 2 Loitering Munition System to Indian Army
Solar Defence and Aerospace Limited (SDAL) delivered its indigenous Nagastra 2 anti-tank loitering
munition system to the Indian Army.
It is a man-portable, suicide-type Unmanned Aerial Vehicle (UAV) designed to strike targets with
precision.
The system has a reported range of 25 km in recover-and-reuse mode and 50 km in fire-and-forget
mode.
Its 4 kg tandem shaped-charge warhead is designed to penetrate up to 650 mm of Rolled
Homogeneous Armour (RHA). It also incorporates features to improve resistance to electronic warfare.

Tom Skerritt, Veteran Hollywood Actor, Passes Away at 93
American actor Tom Skerritt reportedly passed away in October 2026 at the age of 93.
He made his film debut in War Hunt (1962) and appeared in films including M*A*S*H (1970), Alien (1979)
and Top Gun (1986).
He won the 1993 Primetime Emmy Award for Outstanding Lead Actor in a Drama Series for his role in
Picket Fences.
He also co-founded TheFilmSchool in Seattle to support aspiring filmmakers.
International Stuttering Awareness Day 2026 – 22 October
International Stuttering Awareness Day (ISAD) is observed every year on 22 October to promote
understanding and inclusion of people who stutter.
The 2026 theme was “Shades of Stuttering – What’s Yours?”
The day was first observed on 22 October 1998, following international efforts to raise awareness of
stuttering.
The sea-green ribbon, adopted in 2009, represents awareness, support and solidarity.
International Snow Leopard Day 2026 – 23 October
International Snow Leopard Day, also called World Snow Leopard Day, is observed on 23 October to
promote snow leopard conservation and protection of mountain habitats.
The day originated from the Global Snow Leopard Conservation Forum held in Bishkek, Kyrgyzstan, in
2013.
The Bishkek Declaration was adopted by 12 snow leopard range countries in 2013, supporting a
coordinated conservation effort.
The United Nations General Assembly formally proclaimed 23 October as International Snow Leopard
Day through Resolution A/RES/79/143 in December 2024.
Indo-Tibetan Border Police Raising Day 2026 – 24 October
The Indo-Tibetan Border Police (ITBP) observes its Raising Day on 24 October every year.
The force was established in 1962 to strengthen security along the India–Tibet border.
The force operates under the Ministry of Home Affairs and is one of India's Central Armed Police Forces.
Its legal framework includes the ITBP Force Act, 1992, and the ITBP Rules, 1994.
National Cybersecurity Awareness Month 2026 – October
National Cybersecurity Awareness Month is observed every October to promote safe online behaviour
and protect people and organisations from cyber threats.
It was launched in 2004 by the United States Department of Homeland Security and the National
Cybersecurity Alliance.
The National Cybersecurity Alliance's 2026 theme is “Don't Make It Easy for Them”, focusing on
everyday habits that protect personal information and digital accounts.
The Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency's 2026 theme is “Securing the Next 250”, linked to
strengthening cybersecurity as the United States approaches its 250th anniversary.
International Lead Poisoning Prevention Week 2026 – 18–24 October
The 14th International Lead Poisoning Prevention Week was observed from 18 to 24 October 2026.
The 2026 campaign theme was “From Detection to Prevention”.

Agriculture
Agriculture Infrastructure Fund (AIF)
Source: Mint
Context of the News
The Union government has extended the Agriculture Infrastructure Fund (AIF) beyond 30 September
2026, up to 31 March 2027. The Department of Expenditure gave the approval.
What Is the AIF?
A scheme that gives financing support for post-harvest infrastructure
It covers cold storages, warehouses and grading units
Support comes through interest subvention and credit guarantee benefits
What Is the Aim?
Build storage closer to the farm
Reduce crop wastage
Let farmers hold their produce until prices improve, instead of selling immediately after harvest
Important Facts for Exams
AIF extended to 31 March 2027, from the earlier deadline of 30 September 2026
Approved by the Department of Expenditure
Launched in 2020-21; ₹1.03 trillion sanctioned for 2,28,428 projects; over ₹1.75 trillion mobilised
Top states: Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra, Uttar Pradesh, Punjab, Gujarat
The Snow Leopard
Source: Times of India
Context of the News
On International Snow Leopard Day (23 October), the Environment Ministry highlighted India's first Snow
Leopard Population Assessment (SPAI). It recorded 718 snow leopards, of which 477 are in Ladakh.
What Is the Snow Leopard?
Scientific name Panthera uncia
A solitary apex carnivore — it hunts alone and sits at the top of its food chain
Known as the "Ghost of the Mountains", because it is so rarely seen
Adapted to the cold, rugged mountains of Central and South Asia

Farmer IDs Created Under Digital Agriculture Mission
Source: The Hindu
Context of the News
The Union Agriculture Ministry has created 11 crore Farmer IDs — five months ahead of the deadline of 31
March 2027. The milestone was completed on the night of 23 October.
What Is a Farmer ID?
A unique digital identity card for a farmer
Created under the Digital Agriculture Mission
Run by the Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers' Welfare
What Will It Give Access To?
Government schemes
Bank credit
Crop insurance
Disaster relief
Key Terms
Farmer ID: A unique digital identity linking a farmer to their land records and crop details
Digital Agriculture Mission: The government programme building digital infrastructure for farming
AgriStack: The underlying system, built on three registries — farmer identity, land, and crops sown
Verification: Confirming a person's identity and eligibility before a benefit is released
Service delivery: How a government scheme actually reaches the intended person
Important Facts for Exams
11 crore Farmer IDs created, as on 23 October 2026 — five months ahead of the 31 March 2027
deadline
Created under the Digital Agriculture Mission, Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers' Welfare
Union Agriculture Minister: Shivraj Singh Chouhan
Digital Agriculture Mission was approved by the Cabinet in 2024, with an outlay of about ₹2,817 crore
AgriStack's three registries: Farmer Registry, Geo-referenced Village Maps and Crop Sown Registry
The Farmer ID is the identity layer of AgriStack, the equivalent of what Aadhaar is in India Stack
Agriculture is a State subject, so AgriStack implementation varies across states
Enhanced NMMS 2.0 Mobile App for VB-G RAM G
Launch: Union Minister Shivraj Singh Chouhan launched the upgraded National Mobile Monitoring
System (NMMS) 2.0 app in October 2026.
Purpose: Records attendance of workers under the Viksit Bharat-Guarantee for Rozgar & Ajeevika
Mission (Gramin).

Banking and Finance
RBI Revises Bank Leverage Ratio Framework
Announcement: The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) issued the Commercial Banks – Prudential Norms on
Capital Adequacy (Eleventh Amendment Directions), 2026.
Effective Date: 1 April 2027. The changes align the framework with the 2017 Basel Committee on Banking
Supervision standard under Basel III.
Leverage Ratio: Measures a bank’s Tier 1 capital against its total exposure, including on-balance-sheet
and off-balance-sheet items.
Minimum Ratios:
Domestic Systemically Important Banks (D-SIBs): 4%
Indian branches of Global Systemically Important Banks (G-SIBs): 3.5% plus the applicable buffer
Other banks: 3.5%

World Development Report 2026
Source: PIB
Context of the News
The Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology launched the World Bank Group's World
Development Report 2026. The event was organised under the IndiaAI Mission with the World Bank. The
report was first released globally in August 2026.
Key Highlights
Three-step approach to AI: The report recommends adoption, adaptation and advancement.
Developing countries should first use existing AI tools, modify them according to local needs and
gradually build their own AI capabilities.
AI in agriculture: In Baramati, Maharashtra, AI-based farming support reportedly increased
sugarcane yields by 40% while reducing water and fertiliser use by 30%. Farmers received guidance
through voice-based services on mobile phones, making technology accessible even to farmers who
had never used computers.
AI in healthcare: According to Union Minister Ashwini Vaishnaw, AI-based initiatives increased
tuberculosis (TB) detection by 35%.
IndiaAI Mission: The Government of India is developing AI infrastructure, datasets, skills, startups
and safety systems to expand AI use across the country.
Basic infrastructure is essential: Reliable electricity, internet connectivity, computing facilities,
quality data, skilled workers and strong institutions are necessary to make AI useful.
From pilot projects to large-scale use: The report stresses that successful AI experiments should be
properly evaluated and expanded across regions and sectors based on evidence of their impact.

Theoretical Physicist Ashoke Sen Wins Oskar Klein Medal
Award: Ashoke Sen received the 2026 Oskar Klein Medal, becoming the first Indian to win the honour.
Awarding Institutions: Stockholm University and the Nobel Committee for Physics of the Royal Swedish
Academy of Sciences.
Field of Work: His research focuses on string theory, which attempts to explain fundamental particles
and forces, including gravity, within one framework.
Importance: String theory seeks to connect quantum physics with Einstein’s theory of relativity,
although it has not yet produced predictions that can be experimentally confirmed using current
technology.

Science and Technology
Advanced Emergency Braking Systems (AEBS)
Proposal: The Ministry of Road Transport proposed making Advanced Emergency Braking Systems
(AEBS) mandatory for new passenger car models from 1 October 2030. Existing production models
would need to comply by 1 October 2032.
How It Works: The system monitors the road ahead, warns the driver of a possible collision and can
automatically apply the brakes if the driver does not respond in time.
Purpose: To reduce rear-end collisions, protect pedestrians and cyclists, and lower road fatalities.
Key Term: Advanced Driver Assistance Systems (ADAS) refers to vehicle technologies that help drivers
avoid crashes and improve road safety.
NVS-03 Navigation Satellite
The Indian Space Research Organisation (ISRO) is scheduled to launch NVS-03 on 30 October 2026,
using a GSLV Mk II rocket from Sriharikota.
NVS-03 is part of the second-generation Navigation with Indian Constellation (NavIC) satellite series.
Purpose: To replace ageing first-generation navigation satellites and improve India’s independent
satellite-navigation services.
Organisations Involved: ISRO operates NavIC; the Space Applications Centre (SAC) develops
navigation payloads, while the U. R. Rao Satellite Centre (URSC) builds satellite platforms.
Ghostly Eyeless Freshwater Snail Discovered in China
Scientists discovered a new cave-dwelling freshwater snail named Erhaia dijiang in Yunnan Province,
China, at an altitude of 2,062 metres.
Only three living adult specimens were found, raising concerns about the species’ conservation.
Adaptations: It has no eyes, lacks body pigmentation and has a nearly transparent, cone-shaped
shell.
Habitat: The snail lives in shallow cave water among gravel and broken rocks, showing adaptations to
life underground.
Astronomers Detect Magnetic Threads Above the Sun
Astronomers detected fine, thread-like magnetic structures in the Sun’s chromosphere, the layer of its
atmosphere above the visible surface.
The observations were made using the Sunrise-III solar observatory.
Importance: The discovery can help scientists better understand the Sun’s magnetic field and the
physical processes occurring in its atmosphere.
International Days and Observances
United Nations Day – 24 October
Observed annually on 24 October to mark the date the United Nations Charter came into force in 1945.
In 1947, the United Nations General Assembly designated 24 October as United Nations Day.
2026 marked the 81st anniversary of the United Nations.
The day highlights the UN’s role in international peace, cooperation, human rights and development.

World Development Information Day – 24 October
Observed annually on 24 October to raise awareness about global development challenges.
It promotes the sharing of information and international cooperation to support development.
The United Nations General Assembly established the observance in 1972.
The date is linked to the adoption of the International Development Strategy for the Second United
Nations Development Decade in 1970.
Global Media and Information Literacy Week – 24–31 October
Observed from 24 to 31 October to promote media literacy, critical thinking and responsible digital
engagement.
2026 Theme: “We All Play a Part – Media and Information Literacy for the People.”
The initiative was launched in 2012 under the leadership of UNESCO and partner organisations.
It encourages people to assess information carefully and identify misleading or false content.
World Polio Day – 24 October
Observed annually on 24 October to raise awareness about polio and support global eradication
efforts.
2026 Theme: “One day, one focus, Ending polio for good.”
India’s last reported case of wild poliovirus was recorded in Howrah, West Bengal, on 13 January 2011.
The World Health Organization (WHO) certified its South-East Asia Region, including India, as polio-free
on 27 March 2014. India continues vaccination and disease surveillance to prevent the return of polio.

Ramsar Sites in India Reach 103
Source: PIB
Context of the News
India now has 103 Ramsar Sites, covering over 13.87 lakh hectares. The latest additions are Sukhna
Wetland in Chandigarh and Sauj Jheel in Uttar Pradesh.
What Is a Ramsar Site?
A wetland designated as a "Wetland of International Importance"
Under the Ramsar Convention on Wetlands
An intergovernmental treaty signed at Ramsar, Iran, in 1971
What Counts as a Wetland?
A wide definition:
Fens, marshes, peatlands or bodies of water
Natural or artificial
Permanent or temporary
Fresh, brackish or salt
Including marine areas no deeper than six metres at low tide
Recently Added Site
Site Date Detail
11 wetlands 2025 Took the tally to 96; floodplain oxbows, reservoirs and community
reserves
Surha Tal / Jai Prakash
Narayan Bird Sanctuary
June 2026 India's 100th site; Ballia district, Uttar Pradesh; floodplain
ecosystem rich in birdlife
Glaw Lake August 2026 101st; Arunachal Pradesh; an Eastern Himalayan lake with over 150
tree species and 49 orchid species
Sukhna Wetland 15 October 2026 102nd; an artificial rain-fed reservoir in Chandigarh, at the Shivalik
foothills
Sauj Jheel 15 October 2026 103rd; a shallow floodplain lake in Mainpuri, Uttar Pradesh
Important Facts for Exams
India has 103 Ramsar Sites, covering over 13.87 lakh hectares, across 28 States and UTs
100th site: Surha Tal / Jai Prakash Narayan Bird Sanctuary, Ballia, UP, June 2026
India has the third-highest number of Ramsar Sites globally, after the UK and Mexico, and the most in

Hydroseeding: Restoring Mined Land in Jharkhand
Source: New Indian Express
Context of the News
Tata Steel introduced hydroseeding at its West Bokaro Division in Ramgarh district, Jharkhand. It
covers over 50 acres of mined land, nurseries and overburden dumps. This is the first such effort in the
state.
What Is Hydroseeding?
A mechanised planting method
A liquid mixture (slurry) is sprayed over bare ground using high-pressure equipment
What goes into the slurry?
Seeds
Fibrous mulch
Fertiliser
Binders, called tackifiers
Soil-conditioning agents
Water
What Is the Aim?
Quickly establish green cover on damaged land
Strengthen slope stability
Prevent topsoil erosion by wind and water
Reclaim degraded overburden dumps
Without the labour of manual planting
The Camels of Kutch
Source: The Hindu
Context of the News
The camel population in Gujarat’s Kutch district has fallen sharply. From a peak of over 17,600 in 1992,
numbers are now critically low. Attendance at the traditional Bhed Mata Camel Fair has dropped from
thousands to under 200 animals.
What Are They?
Indigenous dromedary camels (Camelus dromedarius)
Native to the salt marshes, coastal mangroves and thorn-forest scrublands of Kutch
Reared by pastoral communities — Rabaris, Maldharis and Jaths

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 176 of 212
“Big scores are built from small daily deposits.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
What Are the Two Breeds?
Breed Habitat
Kharai camel Coastal areas and mangrove swamps. Known as the “swimming camel”
Kutchhi camel Inland thorn-scrub forests, dry grasslands and the rocky Rann of Kutch
Important Facts for Exams
They feed on Avicennia marina mangrove
Reared by the Rabari, Maldhari and Jath communities
Kharai was recognised as a distinct breed by NBAGR in 2015
India has nine registered camel breeds, including Bikaneri, Jaisalmeri, Mewari, Marwari, Kachchhi
and Kharai
India’s camel population has fallen sharply across censuses — it is now around 2.5 lakh
Rajasthan declared the camel its state animal in 2014
2024 was the UN International Year of Camelids
Plant-Based Meat Shows Higher Protein Bioavailability Than Chicken
Study: Scientists from the Council of Scientific and Industrial Research–Institute of Himalayan
Bioresource Technology (CSIR-IHBT), Palampur, and the Good Food Institute India studied protein
bioavailability in plant-based meat and traditional chicken products.
Key Findings: Plant-based seekh kebabs recorded protein bioavailability of 80.8% and 86.1%, compared
with 38.8% for chicken. Plant-based nuggets scored 61.2% against 43.4%, while two plant-based burger
patties scored approximately 57% and 58%, compared with 21.2% for chicken.
Research Method: The researchers used an in vitro method, which studies biological processes outside
a living organism under controlled laboratory conditions.

What Does It Do?
It studies air pollution at a deeper level than normal monitoring:
Identifies the molecular composition of pollutants
Traces how pollutants change chemically in the air
Finds the exact sources in real time
Assesses the health risks they carry

Developed by the Indian Institute of Tropical Meteorology (IITM), Pune
Under the Ministry of Earth Sciences
Located at the IITM Delhi branch

Arabian Black Electric Ray
Source: ThePrint
Context of the News
Scientists from the Zoological Survey of India discovered a new species of deep-sea electric ray in the
southeastern Arabian Sea. It has been named the Arabian Black Electric Ray (Tetronarce
ramanamurthyi).
What Is It?
A newly described deep-sea cartilaginous fish
From the electric ray family Torpedinidae, order Torpediniformes
Named after M. V. Ramana Murthy, Mission Director of the Deep Ocean Mission, for his work in marine
science and ocean engineering
Where Does It Live?
On the deep-water continental shelf and upper bathyal slopes
In the southeastern Arabian Sea, off southwestern India
It lives on the sea floor of the northern Indian Ocean

E100 Flex-Fuel Vehicles in India
Amendment: The Ministry of Road Transport and Highways (MoRTH) amended the Central Motor
Vehicles Rules, 1989, to provide a regulatory framework for flex-fuel vehicles capable of running on
ethanol blends up to E100.
E100 Fuel: E100 refers to fuel consisting almost entirely of ethanol, an alcohol produced through the
fermentation of feedstocks such as sugarcane, molasses and maize.
Flex-Fuel Vehicles (FFVs): These vehicles are designed to operate on petrol, ethanol or different blends
of the two fuels.
Importance: Wider use of ethanol can create additional markets for agricultural produce, reduce
crude oil import dependence and support the decarbonisation of road transport.

AI Neolabs
Meaning: AI Neolabs are independent, research-focused artificial intelligence laboratories established
by leading AI researchers to develop advanced AI systems.
Why in News: Increasing numbers of AI researchers are leaving major technology companies to
establish independent research laboratories.
Main Aim: These laboratories focus on developing safe, reliable and human-aligned artificial
intelligence, including research into safe superintelligence.
Importance: They emphasise long-term scientific research and AI safety rather than focusing only on
immediate commercial returns.

Banking and Finance
Initial Public Offering (IPO)
Meaning: An Initial Public Offering (IPO) is the process through which an unlisted company offers its
shares to the public for the first time and becomes eligible for listing on a stock exchange.
Types: A Fresh Issue involves issuing new shares to raise funds for the company, while an Offer for Sale
(OFS) allows existing shareholders to sell their shares.
Stock Exchanges: In India, companies can list their shares on the Bombay Stock Exchange (BSE) and
the National Stock Exchange (NSE), subject to applicable requirements.
Importance: IPOs help companies raise capital for expansion, while investors get an opportunity to
purchase shares and participate in the company's growth.

Development: The supplied material reports that the India Meteorological Department (IMD) issued a

Storm over the west-central Bay of Bengal.
Origin: The storm reportedly developed from a low-pressure system over the east-central and
adjoining southeast Bay of Bengal.
Classification: A Very Severe Cyclonic Storm is a category in the IMD's classification of tropical

Impact: Such cyclones can bring destructive winds, heavy rainfall, coastal flooding and storm surges.

Agriculture
Nobel Laureate on GM Crops
Source: The Hindu
Context of the News
Richard J. Roberts, the 1993 Nobel laureate in Physiology or Medicine, spoke at the second Emerging
Science, Technology and Innovation Conclave (ESTIC) at Bharat Mandapam. He argued that India can
develop and own GM crop technology itself.
Why Did He Object to the Term "GM"?
He called "genetically modified" very misleading
He preferred "biotechnologically modified" (BM)
His reasoning: "Every life form on this planet has been genetically modified by evolution"
What Is CRISPR?
A gene-editing tool
A guide RNA steers the Cas9 enzyme to a chosen spot in the DNA
The enzyme cuts there, letting scientists alter or insert genes at that point
What Is India's Current Position?
Bt cotton is permitted for commercial cultivation
No GM food crop is approved
Important Facts for Exams
Richard J. Roberts — 1993 Nobel laureate in Physiology or Medicine, for the discovery of split genes.
He spoke at ESTIC 2026, Bharat Mandapam
CRISPR-Cas9 won the 2020 Nobel Prize in Chemistry, for Emmanuelle Charpentier and Jennifer
Doudna
Bt cotton is India's only commercially approved GM crop, since 2002
Bt brinjal moratorium imposed in 2010 by Jairam Ramesh
GM mustard (DMH-11) was cleared by GEAC in 2022; the Supreme Court gave a split verdict in July
2024 and asked for a national policy
Golden Rice was engineered in 1999 by Ingo Potrykus and Peter Beyer
A 2017 PLOS One study found Golden Rice crossed into an Indian variety gave stunted, low-yielding
plants
India's gene-editing position: SDN-1 and SDN-2 crops are exempt from GM rules, since no foreign DNA
is added. SDN-3 remains regulated
India released its first genome-edited rice varieties in 2025 — DRR Dhan 100 (Kamala) and Pusa DST

Rice 1
IUCN Coral Reef Assessment 2026
Source: Down To Earth
Context of the News
The IUCN released its first global assessment of coral reefs under the Red List of Ecosystems, at the UN
Biodiversity Conference in Yerevan, Armenia. It warns that 96 per cent of shallow-water coral reefs face
an extremely high risk of collapse by 2075.
What Are Coral Reefs?
Underwater structures built by coral polyps, tiny animals that secrete calcium carbonate
They live in symbiosis with photosynthetic algae called zooxanthellae
These algae provide up to 90 per cent of the coral's energy and give reefs their colour
Extreme heat stress kills coral, and the reef can be replaced by algae, rubble and sponges
What Are the Main Threats?
Threat Share of reef ecoregions affected
Marine heat stress 77 per cent
Overfishing and fish depletion 63 per cent
Important Facts for Exams
IUCN's first global coral reef assessment under the Red List of Ecosystems, released at the UN
Biodiversity Conference, Yerevan, Armenia
122 of 127 ecoregions (96 per cent) are Critically Endangered; the Western Galápagos is classified
collapsed
24 per cent are critically endangered on observed change alone; 96 per cent when projected
warming is added
They absorb up to 97 per cent of wave energy
Collapse risk is projected by 2075
IUCN — founded 1948, headquartered at Gland, Switzerland
India's coral reefs are found in four main areas: Gulf of Mannar, Gulf of Kachchh, Lakshadweep and
the Andaman and Nicobar Islands
Lakshadweep and the Maldives are atoll formations
Corals are listed under Schedule I of the Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972

SBI Launches CyRIS Cybersecurity Centre of Excellence
State Bank of India (SBI) launched CyRIS, a Cybersecurity Centre of Excellence, in Mumbai in October
2026 to strengthen cybersecurity and improve the financial sector's ability to respond to cyber threats.
The centre was inaugurated by Sanjay Lohiya, Secretary, Department of Financial Services, in the
presence of SBI Chairman C. S. Setty.
CyRIS operates through three pillars: THINK (policy, education and talent development), INNOVATE
(research and security solutions), and DEFEND (threat detection, incident response and cyber
defence).
The initiative aims to strengthen threat intelligence, encourage cybersecurity innovation and improve
the resilience of financial institutions.

RELIEF Initiative
Source: PIB
Context of the News
The Department of Commerce extended the operational window under Component II of the RELIEF
initiative until 31 March 2027.
What Is RELIEF?
Full form: Resilience and Logistics Intervention for Export Facilitation
A time-bound financial and risk-mitigation scheme
It gives exporters a safety net against geopolitical and shipping disruptions
e.g. Bab el-Mandeb and the Strait of Hormuz are the two chokepoints affecting this route. About 12
per cent of global trade passes through the Red Sea
Why Was It Needed?
Launched on 19 March 2026, under the Export Promotion Mission
It responds to:
Rising shipping surcharges
Trade-lane diversions
Transit risks across the Red Sea, the Persian Gulf and wider West Asian shipping routes
Who Runs It?
Formulated by the Department of Commerce
Implemented through the Export Credit Guarantee Corporation of India (ECGC)
Operated through the ECGC digital portal

Miscellaneous General Awareness
Ministry of Culture Signs MoU with France's National Library
On 26 October 2026, the Ministry of Culture signed a Memorandum of Understanding (MoU) with the
Bibliothèque nationale de France (BnF) to preserve and digitise India's manuscript and documentary
heritage.
The partnership brings together the Gyan Bharatam Mission, National Archives of India, National
Library in Kolkata and BnF to identify and digitise Indian-origin manuscripts held in France.
Cooperation will cover digital repositories, conservation technologies, cataloguing standards, Optical
Character Recognition (OCR) and Handwritten Text Recognition (HTR) for Indian scripts.
The Gyan Bharatam Mission focuses on identifying, documenting, conserving, digitising, translating,
researching and sharing India's manuscript heritage.
PM-DAKSH Yojana
PM-DAKSH, launched in 2020–21, provides skill development training to eligible people from Scheduled
Castes, Other Backward Classes, Economically Weaker Sections, De-Notified Tribes and Safai
Karamcharis, including waste pickers.
The scheme targets beneficiaries aged 18–45 years and aims to improve their employability through
skill training.
It is implemented through the National Scheduled Castes Finance and Development Corporation
(NSFDC), National Backward Classes Finance and Development Corporation (NBCFDC) and National
Safai Karamcharis Finance and Development Corporation (NSKFDC).
The supplied figures report 1.87 lakh people trained and 96,162 placed. These figures and the reported
expenditure should be checked against official records before exam use.

The 11th edition of the India–Indonesia joint Special Forces exercise began at Banyuwangi, Indonesia,
on 27 October 2026 and is scheduled to continue until 6 November 2026.
The exercise aims to improve interoperability, coordination and mutual understanding between the
Special Forces of both countries.
India's contingent comprises troops from the Parachute Regiment (Special Forces), while Indonesia is
represented by its Special Forces.
Training focuses on special operations in jungle environments, helping both forces strengthen their
ability to operate together.

NABARD  CURRENT  AFFAIRS
Agriculture
New Genetic Tools for India's Staple Crops
Source: The Hindu
Context of the News
New genetic technologies — CRISPR-Cas9 gene editing and apomixis — are opening ways to improve
India's staple crops, especially rice and wheat. They could produce higher-yielding, climate-resilient
varieties and cut the cost farmers pay for hybrid seed every year.
Why Rice and Wheat?
They occupy more than half of India's cultivated land
They are the main source of food and calories
So improving their productivity matters directly for food security
What Gene-Edited Rice Has India Released?
In 2025, India released two varieties developed using CRISPR-Cas9:
Variety Developed by Main feature
DRR Dhan 100 ICAR-Indian Institute of Rice
Research
Matures in about 130 days against 150 for its parent;
nearly 20 per cent higher yield
Pusa DST Rice 1 ICAR-Indian Agricultural Research
Institute
Performs better in saline and alkaline soils, with 10-30 per
cent higher yield in those conditions
What Is Apomixis?
A form of asexual reproduction in plants
Seeds form without fertilisation
The offspring are genetically almost identical to the mother plant
What Is Happening Elsewhere?
Country Development
Argentina HB4 wheat by Bioceres Crop Solutions — drought-tolerant, using a sunflower gene. This is GM, not
gene editing
Australia Researchers exploring both GM and CRISPR to improve wheat
India Progress in gene-edited rice; no gene-edited wheat released yet
Why Does the GM vs Gene Editing Distinction Matter in India?
Because the two are regulated completely differently:

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 198 of 212
“Attempt what you know first. Speed follows accuracy.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
Category Treatment in India
SDN-1 and SDN-2 gene editing — no foreign DNA added Exempt from GM rules
SDN-3 gene editing — foreign DNA inserted Regulated as GM
Transgenic GM — gene taken from another species Regulated; needs GEAC approval
This is why DRR Dhan 100 and Pusa DST Rice 1 could be released in 2025, while GM mustard remains
stuck in litigation. The difference is not the benefit — it is whether foreign DNA was inserted. HB4 wheat,
using a sunflower gene, falls in the regulated category.
Key Terms
Apomixis: Seed formation without fertilisation, producing offspring genetically similar to the mother
plant
Synthetic apomixis: Using genetic tools to introduce apomixis-like reproduction into crops that
normally reproduce sexually
CRISPR-Cas9: A gene-editing tool that makes precise changes in an organism's own DNA
Hybrid vigour (heterosis): Better growth or yield in offspring produced by crossing genetically
different parents
Gene editing: Changing an organism's existing DNA
Genetic modification (GM): Changing genetic material, often by inserting a gene from another
organism
SDN: Site-Directed Nuclease. SDN-1 makes a cut and lets the plant repair it; SDN-2 guides a small edit
using a template; SDN-3 inserts a whole new gene
Salinity: Excess soluble salts in soil, which harm crop growth
Parthenogenesis: Development of an embryo without fertilisation
Bt cotton remains India's only commercially approved transgenic crop, since 2002
GM mustard (DMH-11) was cleared by GEAC in 2022; the Supreme Court gave a split verdict in July
2024 and asked for a national policy
Khelo India Sports Goods Manufacturing Scheme
The Union Cabinet approved the scheme with an outlay of ₹1,856.67 crore for 2026–27 to 2033–34 to
strengthen India's sports goods manufacturing industry.
Implemented by the Ministry of Youth Affairs and Sports, it aims to promote domestic manufacturing,
investment, research and development, innovation and exports while reducing import dependence.
The scheme has two broad components: manufacturing incentives and ecosystem development
interventions. It also proposes four integrated Sports Goods Manufacturing Zones, each spread over
300 acres or more.
Two major support components have a combined outlay of ₹1,130 crore: 20% capital expenditure
support for eligible anchor investors investing at least ₹100 crore, and turnover-linked incentives of up
to 15% for eligible manufacturers investing at least ₹10 crore.
Two New Plant Species Discovered in Arunachal Pradesh
Researchers from the Botanical Survey of India (BSI) and Madras Christian College discovered two new
flowering plant species in the Vijoynagar region of Changlang district, Arunachal Pradesh.
The species are Ophiorrhiza shidiensis and Ophiorrhiza pemae, belonging to the Rubiaceae family,
commonly known as the coffee family.
Ophiorrhiza shidiensis was named after Shidi, the traditional name for Gandhigram used by the Lisu
(Yobin) community.

Ophiorrhiza pemae was named in honour of Arunachal Pradesh Chief Minister Pema Khandu. The
findings were published in the journal Taiwania.

Banking and Finance
SEBI and Bank of England Sign MoU
On 28 October 2026, the Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) signed a Memorandum of
Understanding with the Bank of England (BoE).
The agreement aims to strengthen regulatory cooperation and information sharing concerning SEBI-
recognised Clearing Corporations (CCs).
It provides a framework for cooperation relating to Central Counterparties (CCPs), allowing the Bank of
England to rely on SEBI's regulatory and supervisory activities, subject to applicable laws.
Clearing corporations help complete trade settlements and reduce the risk of default between buyers
and sellers.
Air India, SBI Card and Visa Partnership
Air India, SBI Card and Visa partnered to develop a co-branded credit card for members of Air India's
Maharaja Club loyalty programme.
The card is expected to offer travel and lifestyle benefits, including Maharaja Points on everyday
purchases and travel transactions.
It is Air India's first co-branded credit card initiative since its privatisation and acquisition by the Tata
Group in January 2022.
The Maharaja Club was redesigned in 2024 and has added more than 5 million members, according
to the supplied information.
Cyberattack on SEBI's SMARTs Portal
SEBI placed its cybersecurity team on red alert following repeated attempts to breach its systems,
including a code execution attack on the SMARTs portal on 26 October 2026.
SMARTs stands for Securities Market Trainers. It is a platform used by eligible trainers to register with
SEBI and conduct investor awareness programmes.
A code execution attack allows an attacker to run unauthorised commands or malicious code on a
computer system by exploiting a vulnerability.
Such attacks can threaten data security and disrupt the functioning of digital platforms.

World Bank Poverty and Equity Brief 2026
Source: The Hindu
Context of the News
The World Bank's October 2026 Poverty and Equity Brief says India has made "remarkable progress" in
reducing poverty, across both income and non-income measures. It calls this one of the fastest
reductions in the world.

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 200 of 212
“Attempt what you know first. Speed follows accuracy.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
What Do the Income Measures Show?
Between 2011 and 2023:
Poverty line 2011 2023
Lower Middle-Income line ($4.20 a day) 57.7 per cent 16.1 per cent
Extreme poverty ($3.00 a day) 27.1 per cent 2.6 per cent
An estimated 496 million people moved out of poverty.
The Bank projects poverty will keep falling, "albeit more gradually".
What Do the Non-Income Measures Show?
Measure Period Figure
OPHI Multidimensional Poverty Index 2005-06 53.8 per cent
OPHI MPI 2019-21 16.4 per cent
World Bank MPI measure 2022-23 15.5 per cent
What Drove the Decline?
The World Bank names three causes:
Strong consumption growth
Rising employment, especially among women
Expanded social transfers, including food subsidies
Important Facts for Exams

India's fiscal deficit reached 42% of the FY27 Budget Estimate during April–September 2026, compared

Dearness Allowance Increased to 63%
The Union Government increased Dearness Allowance (DA) and Dearness Relief (DR) by three
percentage points, from 60% to 63%, with effect from 1 July 2026.
DA is paid to government employees, while DR is paid to pensioners.
It is a cost-of-living adjustment intended to protect the purchasing power of employees and
pensioners against inflation.
The increase helps offset the impact of rising prices on household expenses.
Social Issue in News
Tobacco-Free Youth Campaign 4.0
Union Minister of State Prataprao Jadhav launched the fourth edition of the Tobacco-Free Youth
Campaign (TFYC 4.0) on 28 October 2026 in New Delhi.
The campaign is organised by the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare and will run for 60 days.
Its main objectives are to prevent tobacco use, encourage people to quit tobacco and strengthen
awareness about nicotine addiction.
The campaign places special emphasis on Higher Educational Institutions (HEIs) and creating
tobacco-free campuses. The associated “Say No to Tobacco” pledge encourages young people to
reject tobacco and nicotine products.

Indian Naval Ship INS Sahyadri, an indigenously designed and built guided-missile stealth frigate,
arrived at Busan Naval Harbour, South Korea, to participate in a bilateral maritime exercise.
The exercise involves the Indian Navy and the Republic of Korea Navy (RoKN).
It includes harbour interactions and tactical operations at sea to improve coordination and
interoperability between the two navies.
The exercise aims to strengthen defence cooperation and support a free, open and rules-based
maritime order in the Indo-Pacific region.

Three-Time Asian Para Games Gold Medallist Neeraj Yadav Dies
Indian para-athlete Neeraj Yadav, a three-time Asian Para Games gold medallist, passed away at the
age of 42.
He competed in the F55 category and won gold in men's javelin throw at the 2018 Jakarta Asian Para
Games with an Asian record of 29.84 metres.
At the 2023 Hangzhou Asian Para Games, he won gold medals in men's discus throw and javelin throw.
He had earlier competed in wheelchair tennis before moving to seated para-athletics events.

Agriculture
PI Industries Develops India's First Indigenous Insecticide Molecule
Source: Indian Express
Context of the News
PI Industries Ltd, based in Gurugram, has developed Pioxaniliprole — recognised by the CIB&RC as
India's first indigenously discovered insecticide active ingredient. It will be sold under the brand name
Pioxazon from the Rabi 2026-27 season.
What Makes It a First?
It is the first insecticide active ingredient discovered in India
Developed over more than a decade of research
Which Crops and Pests Is It Approved For?
Crop Pests
Rice Yellow Stem Borer, Leaf Folder, Fall Armyworm
Chilli Fruit Borer, Tobacco Caterpillar
Tomato Fruit Borer
Bengal Gram (Chana) Pod Borer
Important Facts for Exams
Pioxaniliprole is the molecule; Pioxazon is the brand name
Developed by PI Industries Ltd, Gurugram; about ₹2,000 crore and over a decade of research
India's first indigenously discovered insecticide active ingredient
Approved for rice, chilli, tomato and Bengal gram
Class: anthranilic diamide; formulation 20 per cent w/v SC
Mode of action: targets ryanodine receptors, causing uncontrolled calcium release and paralysis
Registered by the CIB&RC under the Insecticides Act, 1968
Other anthranilic diamides already used in India include chlorantraniliprole and cyantraniliprole.
Since all act the same way, cross-resistance is a risk
India is among the world's largest producers and exporters of generic pesticides
India's pesticide use is about 0.5 kg per hectare, against a global average of 2.38 kg
Related: the ban on Paraquat Dichloride, the proposed Carbosulfan ban, and the Pesticides
Management Bill meant to replace the 1968 Act

UNEP Honours India's Ganges Dolphin Recovery
Source: New Indian Express
Context of the News
The UNEP report "Nature's Comeback – Scaling Ecosystem Restoration for People and Planet"
recognised India's Ganges River dolphin recovery programme as one of 35 UN World Restoration
Flagships.
What Is the Ganges River Dolphin?
An obligate freshwater cetacean — it lives only in fresh water
Popularly called Susu, after the sound it makes while breathing
India's National Aquatic Animal
State aquatic animal of Assam
Status
IUCN: Endangered
Schedule I of the Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972
CITES Appendix I
Where Does It Live?
Deep-water pools, river bends and confluence zones
In the Ganges-Brahmaputra-Meghna and Karnaphuli-Sangu river systems
Across India, Nepal and Bangladesh

Source: PIB
Context of the News

What Is It?
A geospatial decision-support app and web portal

informatics
With the National Water Mission, under the Department of Water Resources, River Development and
Ganga Rejuvenation, Ministry of Jal Shakti
What Is the Aim?
Move groundwater recharge planning from ad-hoc decisions to a data-driven, scientific framework
Maximise rainwater harvesting
Improve long-term aquifer sustainability

What Is Cloud Computing?
Computing resources delivered over the internet, on demand
This includes data storage, servers, databases, networking and software
Charged on a pay-as-you-go basis
Instead of buying and running physical servers, an organisation rents capacity as it needs it
What Are the Service Models?
Model What it provides
IaaS — Infrastructure as a Service Basic compute power, storage and networking — virtual machines, block
storage
PaaS — Platform as a Service Development environments, operating systems and database tools, so
developers can build and deploy without managing hardware
SaaS — Software as a Service Ready-to-use software, hosted centrally and used through a browser
Serverless computing The vendor automatically allocates and scales resources. Developers write
code without maintaining servers at all
GSLV-F18 Launches NVS-03 Navigation Satellite
Source: The Hindu
Context of the News
ISRO's GSLV-F18 successfully launched the NVS-03 satellite from the Satish Dhawan Space Centre,
Sriharikota. The satellite is expected to help restore India's NavIC navigation system.
What Is NVS-03?
The third satellite in the next-generation navigation satellite series
It provides continuity of navigation services for the IRNSS series, known as NavIC
What Is NavIC?
India's independent regional navigation satellite system
It provides Position, Navigation and Timing (PNT) service
Coverage: the Indian landmass, plus a region extending about 1,500 km beyond India's boundary
Chola-Era Nataraja and Sivagami Idols Found in Nagapattinam
Source: The Hindu
Context of the News
The Tamil Nadu State Department of Archaeology unearthed bronze alloy idols of Lord Nataraja and
Goddess Sivagami, with their pedestals, during excavations in Nagapattinam.
What Was Found?
Idol Posture Arms Size
Lord Nataraja Ananda Tandava Four 110 cm high, 100 cm wide
Goddess Sivagami Tribhanga Two 82 cm high, 23 cm wide
Found at a depth of one metre
Both came with their pedestals
They are estimated to date to the 12th century AD, in the Chola period.

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 208 of 212
“If it has a number in it, it can be a question.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
What Is Ananda Tandava?
The dance of bliss — the most famous depiction of Shiva as Nataraja, the Lord of Dance.
The standard iconography carries specific meanings:
Upper right hand holds the damaru, the drum of creation
Upper left hand holds agni, fire, representing destruction
Lower right hand is in abhaya mudra, the gesture of protection
Lower left hand points to the raised foot, signifying liberation
He dances on Apasmara, the dwarf of ignorance
The surrounding arch of flames (prabhavali) represents the cosmos
Who Is Sivagami?
Sivakami is Parvati, shown standing beside Nataraja, watching the dance. Tribhanga means the body
bends at three points — neck, waist and knee — a graceful posture common in Indian sculpture.
Important Facts for Exams
Chola bronzes are made by the lost-wax (cire perdue) method, usually in panchaloha
The Chola period, especially under Rajaraja I and Rajendra I, is considered the peak of Indian bronze
sculpture
Great Living Chola Temples are a UNESCO World Heritage Site (1987, extended 2004) — the
Brihadisvara Temple at Thanjavur, the temple at Gangaikonda Cholapuram, and the Airavatesvara
Temple at Darasuram
Nagapattinam was a major Chola port, linked to trade with Southeast Asia. It also had a Buddhist
presence, and the Chudamani Vihara was built there with Srivijaya support
Social Issue
Ayushman Vay Vandana Initiative Completes Two Years
Ayushman Vay Vandana (AVV) completed two years of implementation in October 2026. It provides
healthcare coverage to all senior citizens aged 70 years and above, irrespective of their socio-
economic status.
The initiative is implemented by the National Health Authority (NHA) under the Ministry of Health and
Family Welfare.
Eligible senior citizens receive health coverage of up to ₹5 lakh per family per year for secondary and
tertiary care hospitalisation.
Senior citizens aged 70 years and above who belong to families already covered under Ayushman
Bharat Pradhan Mantri Jan Arogya Yojana (AB PM-JAY) receive an additional top-up cover of up to ₹5
lakh per year, exclusively for themselves.
UN Report: At Least 25 Women and Girls Killed Daily in Armed Conflicts
The 2026 UN Secretary-General’s Report on Women, Peace and Security (WPS) revealed that at least
25 women and girls were killed daily in armed conflicts worldwide during 2025.
More than 37,000 civilians were killed in armed conflicts in 2025, with women and girls accounting for
25% of the deaths.
The United Nations verified 9,788 cases of conflict-related sexual violence in 2025. Around 750 million
women and girls lived within 50 km of active conflicts.
Around 170 million women and girls faced conflict and extreme poverty, surviving on less than USD 3

October 2026 full compilation  ·  Page 209 of 212
“If it has a number in it, it can be a question.”
WhatsApp channel: whatsapp.com/channel/0029Vao03aUADTO9sOoigE2F  ·  +91 87086 52887  ·  c4scourses.in  ·  free, share it on
per day. Women also spent five times as many hours on unpaid care work as men in conflict-affected
countries.
Background
On 31 October 2000, the United Nations Security Council adopted Resolution 1325, establishing the
Women, Peace and Security Agenda.
The agenda promotes women's participation in conflict prevention, peace negotiations and post-
conflict reconstruction.
The UN Secretary-General publishes an annual report to review progress under this agenda.
World Stroke Day – 29 October
World Stroke Day is observed annually on 29 October to raise awareness about stroke prevention,
treatment and its impact.
2026 Theme: “Every M1nute Counts.”
The day was established in 2004, and the first global observance took place on 29 October 2006.
The World Stroke Organization (WSO) was formed in October 2006.
Important Days
World Savings Day 2026 – 31 October
World Savings Day (WSD), also known as World Thrift Day, is observed annually on 31 October to
promote saving habits and financial security.
2026 Theme: “AI Advises, Human Decides.”
It was introduced by Italian Professor Filippo Ravizza.
The first World Savings Day was observed on 31 October 1924 during the first International Thrift
Congress in Milan, Italy.
World Cities Day 2026 – 31 October
World Cities Day (WCD) is observed annually on 31 October to promote international cooperation on
urbanisation and sustainable urban development.
2026 Theme: “Regenerating the City: Adequate Housing for All.”
The United Nations General Assembly designated the day through Resolution 68/239 (2013). The first
global observance took place in 2014.
Fuzhou, China, is hosting the 2026 global observance, focusing on neighbourhood renewal and
sustainable urban development.

India Discovers New Microscopic Land Snail Species in Maharashtra
Researchers from the Thackeray Wildlife Foundation discovered a new microscopic land snail species
named Dicharax tilariensis in the Tilari region of Kolhapur district, Maharashtra.
The species is currently known only from the Tilari region, making it endemic to the area.
It inhabits moist soil, forest litter and decaying organic matter in the semi-evergreen forests of Tilari.
Specimens were collected near Swapnavel Point, close to Tilarinagar, at an elevation of 700–800
metres above sea level.
New Mollisia Fungus Discovered in the Sikkim Himalaya
Microbiologists discovered a new fungal species named Mollisia sokpayensia in the Sikkim Himalaya in
October 2026.
The research team was led by Manipur scientist Dr Laishram Shantikumar Singh and included
Subecha Rai and Gajanan V.
The discovery adds a new species to the known fungal diversity of the Himalayan region.
Impacts of Volcanic Ash on Aviation and Public Health
Volcanic ash consists of fine fragments of rocks, minerals and volcanic glass released during volcanic
eruptions.
It can travel long distances from the eruption site and remain suspended in the atmosphere.
Volcanic ash can damage aircraft engines and other aircraft components because of its abrasive
nature.
It can also affect public health, particularly by causing respiratory problems when inhaled.
